If there are no unstaged changes, check for staged changes with `git diff --cached`. If the user specified a commit range, use that instead.\n33\t\n34\tWrite the diff to a temp file for pal consumption:\n35\t\n36\t```bash\n37\tdiff_file=$(mktemp /tmp/kk-review-code-XXXXXXXX.patch)\n38\tgit diff > \"$diff_file\" && wc -l \"$diff_file\"\n39\t```\n40\t\n41\tAdjust the `git diff` command to match the actual scope (staged, unstaged, or commit range). The temp file is passed to pal via `relevant_files` in Step 2 and should be cleaned up after the review completes (`rm \"$diff_file\"`).\n42\t\n43\t**Edge cases:**\n44\t\n45\t- **No changes**: Inform the user and stop.\n46\t- **Large diff (>500 lines)**: Proceed \u2014 the sub-agent handles batching internally. If the diff exceeds the sub-agent's context window, note the limitation and suggest the user scope the review to specific files or tasks.\n47\t\n48\t### 1b) Locate spec context\n49\t\n50\tSpec context is optional but improves review quality:\n51\t\n52\t1. If this review is happening within `/kk:implement`, locate the relevant `design.md` section and task description from `tasks.md` in the feature's `/docs/feat/wip/[feature]/` directory.\n53\t2. If standalone, check if the user provided context or if design docs exist in `/docs/feat/wip/` that relate to the changed files.\n54\t3. If no spec context is found, that's fine \u2014 the sub-agent works without it.\n55\t\n56\tCapture the relevant spec excerpt (design rationale, task description, documented decisions) as text to inject into the sub-agent prompt.\n57\t\n58\t### 1c) Detect active profiles and resolve checklists\n59\t\n60\tDelegate to [shared-profile-detection.md](shared-profile-detection.md) with the diff from Step 1a as input. The procedure returns a list of records \u2014 one per matched profile \u2014 each naming the trigger signal and the files that activated it.\n61\t\n62\tFor each active profile, resolve the checklists to apply:\n63\t\n64\t1. Read `${TOOLBOX_PLUGIN_ROOT}/profiles/<profile>/review-code/index.md`.\n65\t2. Collect every entry under **Always load**.\n66\t3. For every **Load if:** conditional entry, evaluate the predicate against the diff; collect the entry when it matches.\n67\t\n68\tAccumulate a flat list of `(profile, checklist, triggered_by)` records \u2014 carry the `triggered_by` signal from each detection record through to every checklist resolved for that profile. This list \u2014 not any hardcoded category sequence \u2014 is what the sub-agent and pal prompts will receive in Step 2. If no profile matched, the list is empty; both reviewers fall back to general guidance.\n69\t\n70\t### 1d) Resolve pal model\n71\t\n72\tCall `pal` `listmodels` to get available models. Select the most capable model (prefer latest generation with thinking/reasoning support) for the `pal` codereview call in Step 2.\n73\t\n74\t### 1e) Curate rejected approaches\n75\t\n76\tBefore spawning sub-agents, prepare a brief summary of approaches that were tried and failed during implementation. Keep it to concrete facts (\"approach X caused regression Y\"), not the full debugging narrative. If no approaches were rejected, skip this.\n77\t\n78\t### 1f) Determine task scope\n79\t\n80\tBuild the Task Scope artifact following [shared-review-scope-protocol.md](shared-review-scope-protocol.md). This is what prevents reviewers from flagging pending tasks as missing functionality \u2014 it is not optional when a feature directory is present.\n81\t\n82\t- **Invoked from `/kk:implement`**: the feature directory and current task are known. Read `tasks.md`, list the current task (plus any other `done` tasks) as in-scope and any `pending`/`in-progress` tasks as out-of-scope. Use mode `mid-implementation` unless all tasks are `done`.\n83\t- **Invoked directly inside a feature**: locate the relevant `/docs/feat/wip/[feature]/tasks.md`. Classify by status field. Use `post-implementation` only when every task is `done`.\n84\t- **No feature directory relates to the diff**: emit the \"No task scope available\" variant from the shared protocol and proceed.\n85\t\n86\tThe resulting block is inlined into both reviewer prompts in Step 2.\n87\t\n88\t### 1g) Collect context files for pal\n89\t\n90\tAssemble the file list that will be passed to pal codereview via `relevant_files`. All paths must be absolute.\n91\t\n92\t1. **Diff file** \u2014 the temp file written in Step 1a.\n93\t2. **Changed source files** \u2014 every file touched by the diff (from `git diff --stat`).\n94\t3. **Surrounding code files** \u2014 files a reviewer needs to verify cross-file correctness. Collect in priority order, stop at **10 files**:\n95\t   1. Files directly imported by the changed files (type definitions, interfaces, contracts).\n96\t   2. Direct callers of changed public functions/methods (one level up only).\n97\t   3. Adjacent files in the same package/module that share types or conventions with the changed code.\n98\t      Use `grep`/`rg` on import statements and function names to locate these. If more than 10 candidates emerge, keep only categories 1\u20132. If fewer than 3, that's fine \u2014 small diffs legitimately have few dependencies.\n99\t4. **Profile checklist files** \u2014 the absolute paths of every resolved `(profile, checklist)` file from Step 1c. These give pal the same domain-specific review criteria as Reviewer A.\n100\t5. **Design/implementation docs** \u2014 if spec context was located in Step 1b, include the full `design.md` and `implementation.md` file paths (not excerpts). These enable pal to flag spec deviations.\n101\t\n102\tStore this list for use in Step 2 (Reviewer B).\n103\t\n104\t---\n105\t\n106\t## Step 2: Spawn Reviewers (Parallel)\n107\t\n108\tLaunch both reviewers in a **single message** so they execute in parallel.\n109\t\n110\t### Reviewer A \u2014 `code-reviewer` sub-agent\n111\t\n112\tThe `code-reviewer` sub-agent cannot resolve the plugin root on its own (it has no shell). Resolve it yourself \u2014 you already read checklist paths under it in Step 1c \u2014 and inject the absolute value into the `## Plugin Root` section of the prompt below, so the sub-agent can open the checklist files. Expand plugin-relative checklist paths to absolute paths before passing the prompt to the sub-agent.\n113\t\n114\tSpawn using the Agent tool with:\n115\t\n116\t| Parameter       | Value                     |\n117\t| --------------- | ------------------------- |\n118\t| `subagent_type` | `kk:code-reviewer`        |\n119\t| `description`   | `Isolated code review`    |\n120\t| `prompt`        | See prompt template below |\n121\t\n122\t**Sub-agent prompt template:**\n123\t\n124\t```\n125\tYou are reviewing the following code changes. Apply your full review workflow.\n126\t\n127\t## Plugin Root\n128\t\n129\t{the absolute plugin-root path resolved in Step 1c}\n130\t\n131\t## Git Diff\n132\t\n133\t{paste the git diff_file path here}\n134\t\n135\t## Active Profiles and Resolved Checklists\n136\t\n137\t{list of (profile, checklist, triggered_by) records from Step 1c, formatted as a flat tuple list \u2014 one record per line:\n138\t- profile: <name>, checklist: <checklist_filename>, triggered_by: <signal_type> \u2014 <signal_description>\n139\t- profile: <name>, checklist: <checklist_filename>, triggered_by: <signal_type> \u2014 <signal_description>\n140\t...\n141\t}\n142\t\n143\tFor each record, read the checklist at `${TOOLBOX_PLUGIN_ROOT}/profiles/<profile>/review-code/<checklist>` and apply it to the diff. If no profiles are active (empty list), fall back to general review guidance without profile-specific checklists.\n144\t\n145\t## Spec Context\n146\t\n147\t{spec excerpt from Step 1b, or \"No spec context available \u2014 review based on code quality alone.\"}\n148\t\n149\t{Task Scope block from Step 1f \u2014 either the populated scope artifact or the \"No task scope available\" variant}\n150\t\n151\t## Rejected Approaches\n152\t\n153\t{curated rejected approaches from Step 1e, or \"No rejected approaches to note.\"}\n154\t\n155\tProduce your findings in the output format specified in your agent definition.\n156\t```\n157\t\n158\t### Reviewer B \u2014 `pal` codereview\n159\t\n160\tFollow the invocation protocol in [shared-pal-codereview-invocation.md](shared-pal-codereview-invocation.md).\n161\t\n162\t**`step` parameter (step 1):** Keep lean \u2014 framing + scope + spec summary + file manifest:\n163\t\n164\t1. Framing: \"Review the following code changes for correctness, security, architecture, and adherence to design intent. The diff, source files, review checklists, and design docs are provided as files.\"\n165\t2. Task Scope block from Step 1f.\n166\t3. One-paragraph spec context summary from Step 1b (or \"No spec context available \u2014 review based on code quality alone.\").\n167\t4. File manifest \u2014 categorized list of the paths in `relevant_files` so pal knows each file's role (see [shared-pal-codereview-invocation.md](shared-pal-codereview-invocation.md) \u00a7`step` content for format).\n168\t\n169\tDo NOT inline the diff or file contents into `step`.\n170\t\n171\t**`relevant_files` parameter:** Use the file list assembled in Step 1g.\n172\t\n173\t**`model` parameter:** Use the model resolved in Step 1d.\n174\t\n175\t### Parallel execution\n176\t\n177\tIssue the pal step 1 call and the Agent tool call (Reviewer A) in the **same message** so they execute in parallel. When both return, make the pal step 2 continuation call using the `continuation_id` from step 1.\n178\t\n179\t### Error handling\n180\t\n181\tHandle reviewer failures inline as they occur:\n182\t\n183\t- **`pal` failure** (listmodels returns no models, or codereview step 1/2 fails): Note the failure, proceed to Step 3 with code-reviewer findings only.\n184\t- **`code-reviewer` sub-agent failure** (timeout or error): Note the failure, proceed to Step 3 with pal findings only. Suggest `/kk:review-code` (standard mode) as supplement.\n185\t- **Both reviewers fail**: Abort isolated mode. Display message suggesting fallback to `/kk:review-code` (standard mode). Do not proceed to Step 3.\n186\t- **Malformed output**: Attempt best-effort parsing. If completely unparseable, treat as a failure and apply the rules above.\n187\t\n188\t---\n189\t\n190\t## Step 3: Annotate Findings\n191\t\n192\tThe main agent performs annotation \u2014 providing context, not judgment. Do NOT assign dispositions (Confirmed, Disputed, etc.). The user is the final arbiter.\n193\t\n194\t### 3a) Duplicate merging\n195\t\n196\tCompare findings from both reviewers by file location and issue description:\n197\t\n198\t- When both flag the same logical issue: merge into one entry, tag as **\"corroborated\"** \u2014 independent confirmation from different models is high signal.\n199\t- Severity stays as each reviewer assessed it. If they disagree on severity, show both assessments side by side.\n200\t- If only one reviewer flagged an issue, keep it as-is with reviewer attribution.\n201\t\n202\t### 3b) Author context annotations\n203\t\n204\tFor each finding, consider whether the implementation session context adds relevant information:\n205\t\n206\t- If yes: add a clearly-labeled **\"Author context\"** annotation explaining the decision (e.g., \"I chose bcrypt cost 10 because benchmarks showed cost 12 added 400ms\").\n207\t- If no: leave the finding as-is \u2014 not every finding needs an annotation.\n208\t- Annotations are context, not judgments. \"I chose X because Y\" is correct. \"This finding is invalid\" is **not**.\n209\t\n210\t### 3c) Author-sourced findings\n211\t\n212\tIf the close re-reading during annotation triggers new observations, add them:\n213\t\n214\t- Tag as **\"author-sourced\"** \u2014 clearly distinct from sub-agent findings.\n215\t- The user knows these come from the author and can weight accordingly.\n216\t\n217\t### 3d) pal follow-up (optional)\n218\t\n219\tIf a pal finding is ambiguous or unclear, the main agent MAY use pal's follow-up interaction capability to clarify before presenting to the user.\n220\t\n221\t---\n222\t\n223\t## Step 4: Index Findings\n224\t\n225\tIndex any P0/P1 findings that suggest a systemic or structural pattern (not isolated typos or one-off mistakes) as `kk:review-findings`. Index on first encounter \u2014 recurrence detection happens on the search side in future reviews. This applies to findings from any source \u2014 corroborated, single-reviewer, or author-sourced.\n226\t\n227\t- If no P0/P1 systemic findings exist, explicitly note \"No findings to index\" and move on.\n228\t- This step is mandatory \u2014 do not skip it even if the review found no issues.\n229\t\n230\t---\n231\t\n232\t## Step 5: Present Report\n233\t\n234\tUse this report template, organized by agreement level:\n235\t\n236\t```markdown\n237\t## Review Summary (Isolated Mode)\n238\t\n239\t**Reviewers**: code-reviewer (Claude sub-agent), pal codereview ([model name])\n240\t**Files reviewed**: X files, Y lines changed\n241\t\n242\t---\n243\t\n244\t### Corroborated Findings\n245\t\n246\t(Both reviewers flagged \u2014 highest signal)\n247\t\n248\t- **[file:line]** Brief title \u27e8corroborated\u27e9\n249\t  - Profile: {profile_name} \u00b7 Checklist: {checklist_filename}\n250\t  - Triggered by: {signal_type} \u2014 {signal_description}\n251\t  - code-reviewer: [severity] \u2014 [description]\n252\t  - pal: [description in native format]\n253\t  - Author context: [optional annotation]\n254\t\n255\t### Code Reviewer Findings\n256\t\n257\t(code-reviewer sub-agent only \u2014 P0-P3 format)\n258\t\n259\t- **[file:line]** Brief title\n260\t  - Profile: {profile_name} \u00b7 Checklist: {checklist_filename}\n261\t  - Triggered by: {signal_type} \u2014 {signal_description}\n262\t  - Severity: P[0-3] | Confidence: [X]%\n263\t  - [description and suggested fix]\n264\t  - Author context: [optional annotation]\n265\t\n266\t### External Review Findings\n267\t\n268\t(pal codereview \u2014 native format)\n269\t\n270\t- [pal output presented in its native format]\n271\t  - Profile: {profile_name} \u00b7 Checklist: {checklist_filename}\n272\t  - Triggered by: {signal_type} \u2014 {signal_description}\n273\t  - Author context: [optional annotation]\n274\t\n275\t### Author-Sourced Findings\n276\t\n277\t(Main agent observations during annotation \u2014 weight accordingly)\n278\t\n279\t- **[file:line]** Brief title \u27e8author-sourced\u27e9\n280\t  - Profile: generic \u00b7 Checklist: \u2014\n281\t  - Triggered by: \u2014\n282\t  - [description]\n283\t```\n284\t\n285\t**Section rules:**\n286\t\n287\t- Omit any section that has no findings (e.g., if no corroborated findings, skip that section).\n288\t- If a reviewer failed and only one reviewer's findings are present, note the failure at the top and present the available findings under the appropriate section.\n289\t\n290\t### Next steps\n291\t\n292\tAfter presenting the report, ask the user how to proceed:\n293\t\n294\t```markdown\n295\t---\n296\t\n297\t## Next Steps\n298\t\n299\tI found X issues (corroborated: ..., code-reviewer: ..., pal: ..., author-sourced: ...).\n300\t\n301\tThe actionable items I recommend fixing:\n302\t\n303\t1. P1 (corroborated) ...\n304\t2. P2 (code-reviewer) ...\n305\t3. P2 (pal) ...\n306\t4. P3 (corroborated) ...\n307\t5. P3 (author-sourced) ...\n308\t\n309\tItems I recommend keeping as is:\n310\t\n311\t- ...: the finding is a false-positive because ...\n312\t- ...: correct behavior because ...\n313\t- ...: mirrors production behavior of ...\n314\t\n315\t**How would you like to proceed?**\n316\t\n317\t1. **Fix all** \u2014 I'll implement all suggested fixes\n318\t2. **Fix corroborated + high severity** \u2014 Address corroborated findings and P0/P1 issues\n319\t3. **Fix specific items** \u2014 Tell me which issues to fix\n320\t4. **No changes** \u2014 Review complete, no implementation needed\n321\t\n322\tPlease choose an option or provide specific instructions.\n323\t```\n324\t\n325\t**Important**: Do NOT implement any changes until the user explicitly confirms. This is a review-first workflow.\n326\t\n327\t---\n328\t\n329\t## Step 6: Verify Outputs\n330\t\n331\tBefore declaring the review complete, check each item in the **Required Outputs** section of SKILL.md:\n332\t\n333\t- [ ] Review report presented to user\n334\t- [ ] P0/P1 systemic findings indexed as `kk:review-findings` (or explicitly noted \"No findings to index\")\n335\t- [ ] Next steps confirmation from user\n336\t\n337\tIf any item is unchecked, go back and complete it before proceeding.\n338\t"
  }

Detects SOLID violations, security risks, and proposes actionable improvements.\n5\t  Use when performing code reviews.\n6\t---\n7\t\n8\t# SOLID Code Review\n9\t\n10\t## Overview\n11\t\n12\tPerform a structured review of the current git changes with focus on SOLID, architecture, removal candidates, and security risks. Default to review-only output unless the user asks to implement changes.\n13\t\n14\t## Conventions\n15\t\n16\t- **Read capy knowledge base conventions** at [shared-capy-knowledge-protocol.md](shared-capy-knowledge-protocol.md).\n17\t- **Read profile detection** \u2014 the set of reference checklists loaded for a given diff \u2014 at [shared-profile-detection.md](shared-profile-detection.md). The workflow below invokes it in Step 2 and uses the resulting `(profile, checklist)` list to drive Steps 3\u20134.\n18\t\n19\t## Required Outputs\n20\t\n21\tBefore declaring the review complete, verify all outputs are delivered:\n22\t\n23\t- [ ] Review report presented to user\n24\t- [ ] P0/P1 systemic findings indexed as `kk:review-findings` (skip if no qualifying findings)\n25\t- [ ] Next steps confirmation from user\n26\t\n27\tIndexing is owned by this skill \u2014 callers (e.g., `/kk:implement`) do NOT duplicate it.\n28\t\n29\t## Review Modes\n30\t\n31\t### Standard Mode (`/kk:review-code`)\n32\t\n33\tReviews code in the main conversation context. Fast, single-pass review using the workflow below.\n34\t\n35\t### Isolated Mode (`/kk:review-code:isolated`)\n36\t\n37\tDelegates detection to independent reviewers that did not write the code, then annotates their findings with author context. Two parallel reviewers: a `code-reviewer` sub-agent and `pal codereview` (external model in native format). Produces a report organized by agreement level with corroborated findings highlighted.\n38\t\n39\t- **Cost**: Higher (sub-agent + external model + annotation)\n40\t- **Isolation**: True \u2014 reviewers have zero authorship bias or session context\n41\t- **Degradation**: Graceful \u2014 if one reviewer fails, proceeds with the other; if both fail, suggests standard mode fallback\n42\t- **Best for**: When extra rigor is worth the cost (pre-merge, high-stakes changes)\n43\t\n44\tSee [review-isolated.md](./review-isolated.md) for the isolated workflow.\n45\t\n46\t## Severity Levels\n47\t\n48\t| Level  | Name     | Description                                                      | Action                             |\n49\t| ------ | -------- | ---------------------------------------------------------------- | ---------------------------------- |\n50\t| **P0** | Critical | Security vulnerability, data loss risk, correctness bug          | Must block merge                   |\n51\t| **P1** | High     | Logic error, significant SOLID violation, performance regression | Should fix before merge            |\n52\t| **P2** | Medium   | Code smell, maintainability concern, minor SOLID violation       | Fix in this PR or create follow-up |\n53\t| **P3** | Low      | Style, naming, minor suggestion                                  | Optional improvement               |\n54\t\n55\t## Workflow\n56\t\n57\t### Mandatory ordering \u2014 methodology before evidence\n58\t\n59\tThe workflow below is strictly sequential. **Do not read the diff's contents, re-read changed files, run `capy_search`, or begin forming findings until you have completed profile detection and loaded every resolved checklist file.** Until then, your only contact with the changes is `git diff --stat` (filenames only) \u2014 enough to drive profile detection, but not enough to pattern-match findings.\n60\t\n61\tThis ordering is load-bearing, not stylistic. Reviewing from a diff before loading profile checklists is the known failure mode this skill is designed to prevent: the LLM has enough from the diff to produce plausible findings, and optimizes away the methodology if the workflow permits.\n62\t\n63\t**Phases** (summary \u2014 the detailed procedure in [review-process.md](./review-process.md) breaks presentation into three distinct numbered steps: present results, next-steps confirmation, verify outputs):\n64\t\n65\t1. Scope \u2014 `git diff --stat` for filenames only (no content reads)\n66\t2. Detect active profiles \u2014 delegate to `shared-profile-detection.md`; produce the list of `(profile, checklist)` records\n67\t3. Load profile review indexes \u2014 for each active profile, resolve its `review-code/index.md`; collect always-load + filename-evaluable conditionals now; defer content-evaluable conditionals to Step 6\n68\t4. Read resolved checklists \u2014 read every `(profile, checklist)` file collected in Step 3 into context\n69\t5. Read the diff and re-read changed files \u2014 now, with methodology loaded; also run `capy_search` for `kk:review-findings` and `kk:lang-idioms`\n70\t6. Resolve content-evaluable conditional entries \u2014 for each deferred entry from Step 3, evaluate the predicate against the file content read in Step 5; load any newly-matching checklists into context\n71\t7. Apply checklists \u2014 iterate the full resolved list (Steps 3 + 6); emit findings grouped by `(profile, checklist)`\n72\t8. Self-check and confidence assessment\n73\t9. Index findings \u2014 capy index systemic P0/P1 patterns as `kk:review-findings`\n74\t10. Present results with next steps\n75\t\n76\tSee [review-process.md](./review-process.md) for the detailed step-by-step process.\n77\t\n78\t## Invocation\n79\t\n80\tStandard mode:\n81\t\n82\t```\n83\t/kk:review-code\n84\t```\n85\t\n86\tIsolated mode with independent sub-agents:\n87\t\n88\t```\n89\t/kk:review-code:isolated\n90\t```\n91\t"

EVENT e40.0
{
  "id": "e40.0",
  "source_sequence": 40,
  "session_id": "bcb47d65-8609-4412-b2b2-ea3dba438aa6",
  "actor": "main",
  "parent_tool_use_id": null,
  "block": {
    "tool_use_id": "toolu_01CLZntNt3GbWrxA3aBxnepN",
    "type": "tool_result",
    "content": "1\t## Profile detection procedure\n2\t\n3\tSingle source of truth for computing the set of profiles active in the current context.\n4\tConsumed by six skills: `/kk:review-code`, `/kk:review-spec`, `/kk:design`, `/kk:implement`, `/kk:test`, and `/kk:document`.\n5\t\n6\tEvery profile under `klaude-plugin/profiles/<name>/` declares its own trigger rule in `DETECTION.md` using the mandatory three-section schema (`## Path signals`, `## Filename signals`, `## Content signals`).\n7\tThe shared procedure below applies the same algorithm against every profile's declared values.\n8\t\n9\t### Inputs per consuming skill\n10\t\n11\tNot every consumer has a diff available. Use the input listed for your skill:\n12\t\n13\t- **`/kk:review-code`** \u2014 git diff (staged, or an explicit commit range). Scope is\n14\t  the set of files the diff touches.\n15\t- **`/kk:review-spec`** \u2014 git diff when invoked standalone; the feature directory's\n16\t  full file list when invoked by `/kk:implement` (spec review runs over the whole\n17\t  feature, not just the current task's diff).\n18\t- **`/kk:test`** \u2014 git diff mid-feature, OR the feature directory's file list\n19\t  post-implementation.\n20\t- **`/kk:implement`** \u2014 the current sub-task's target file list, augmented by the\n21\t  diff accumulated so far in the feature.\n22\t- **`/kk:design`** \u2014 **no file list available** (implementation does not yet exist).\n23\t  Detection uses a user-declared or keyword-inferred signal instead; see\n24\t  [The `/kk:design` interaction pattern](#the-design-interaction-pattern) below.\n25\t- **`/kk:document`** \u2014 feature directory's current file list; diff optional.\n26\t\n27\t### The `/kk:design` interaction pattern\n28\t\n29\tThe design phase runs before any code exists, so file-based detection is impossible. Detection uses idea-prose keyword matching against tokens declared in each profile's `DETECTION.md`.\n30\t\n31\t**Algorithm:**\n32\t\n33\t1. **Collect tokens.** Iterate \u00a7Known profiles. For each `<name>`, `Read` `${TOOLBOX_PLUGIN_ROOT}/profiles/<name>/DETECTION.md`. If the file has no `## Design signals` section, skip \u2014 that profile does not participate in design-phase detection. Otherwise, parse `display_name` and `tokens` from the section.\n34\t2. **Build union.** Collect all declared tokens into a single set, each tagged by its source profile name and `display_name`.\n35\t3. **Match.** Check the idea prose against the union. Matching is case-insensitive, whole-word (so `pod` in \"podcast\" does not fire).\n36\t4. **Confirm.** On match, surface a confirmation prompt per matched profile:\n37\t   *\"This appears to be a {display_name} feature. Activate the {profile_name} profile?\"* \u2014 let the user confirm yes/no. When multiple profiles match, confirm each independently.\n38\t5. **Fallback.** If no token matches but the idea is **ambiguous** \u2014 names infrastructure, deployment, runtime, or platform concerns without naming a specific technology (e.g., _\"add a caching layer for the service\"_, _\"build a CI pipeline\"_, _\"deploy to production\"_); or includes overloaded tokens that collide across domains \u2014 build the fallback prompt dynamically from all profiles that declare `## Design signals`:\n39\t   *\"Does this feature involve {display_name_1, display_name_2, ...}? If yes, which?\"*\n40\t\n41\tConfirmation is required \u2014 the /kk:design skill never auto-activates a profile silently. The narrow per-profile token sets avoid noisy false positives from tokens that overload across domains.\n42\t\n43\tOnce activated, subsequent design-phase steps treat the profile as active in the same record shape produced by file-based detection (see \u00a7Output shape).\n44\t\n45\t### Known profiles\n46\t\n47\tThis is the authoritative enumeration of profile `<name>`s \u2014 do NOT try discover profiles via any other means.\n48\tAn explicit list is boring, deterministic, and unambiguous; runtime filesystem enumeration against the plugin tree has proven unreliable.\n49\t\n50\t- `go`\n51\t- `python`\n52\t- `java`\n53\t- `js_ts`\n54\t- `kotlin`\n55\t- `k8s`\n56\t- `k8s-operator`\n57\t- `skill-md`\n58\t\n59\t### Algorithm\n60\t\n61\tThis procedure reads files under the plugin root. The main agent resolves the plugin root from its shell variable `$TOOLBOX_PLUGIN_ROOT`; a Read-only sub-agent uses the absolute plugin-root path injected into its prompt under `## Plugin Root` (see its agent definition). Substitute that resolved path for the plugin-root prefix in every `\u2026/profiles/\u2026` read below.\n62\t\n63\t1. **Iterate profiles.** For each \u00a7Known profiles `<name>`:\n64\t   1. Use the `Read` tool on `${TOOLBOX_PLUGIN_ROOT}/profiles/<name>/DETECTION.md`.\n65\t   2. If `Read` fails with ENOENT (profile name in list but directory missing \u2014 a stale list entry), skip silently and move on.\n66\t   3. If `Read` succeeds, parse the declared `## Path signals`, `## Filename signals`, and `## Content signals` sections.\n67\t\n68\t2. **Evaluate in cost order.** For each input file, check signals in this order: path \u2192 filename \u2192 content. Cheapest first.\n69\t3. **Apply the authority rule.** A file activates the profile only if a **filename signal** OR **content signal** matches.\n70\t   A path-only match does NOT activate. Paths are a pre-filter that promotes files to \"candidates\"; authoritative activation requires filename or content confirmation.\n71\t   A file that matches NO path signal is still evaluated against filename and content signals \u2014 path pre-filtering is a cost hint, not a gate.\n72\t   (Otherwise a `Chart.yaml` at a non-standard path would be missed.)\n73\t4. **Bound content inspection.** Read at most ~16 KB per file when evaluating content signals.\n74\t   Multi-document YAML is inspected per `---`-separated block \u2014 a file may have five blocks, and only the third need match for the file to activate the profile.\n75\t5. **Collect records.** Accumulate one record per matched profile with the triggering files and the signal descriptions that fired.\n76\t\n77\t### Tool choice\n78\t\n79\t- Single file at `${TOOLBOX_PLUGIN_ROOT}/\u2026` \u2192 `Read`. This is what the algorithm uses.\n80\t- Enumeration across profiles \u2192 iterate the \u00a7Known profiles list, `Read` each. Never `Glob` (cwd-scoped, misses outside-cwd paths).\n81\t\n82\t### Two dimensions: cost vs authority\n83\t\n84\tSignals live on two axes that point in different directions. Keep them separate in your mental model:\n85\t\n86\t- **Evaluation cost** (cheapest first): path < filename < content.\n87\t  Path globs touch only the path string;\n88\t  filename matches are exact string compares;\n89\t  content inspection opens the file.\n90\t- **Authority** (most authoritative first): filename \u2248 content > path.\n91\t  A filename or content match activates the profile; a path-only match does not.\n92\t  Filename and content are equally authoritative, but filename resolves first at runtime \u2014 a filename match short-circuits content inspection for that file.\n93\t\n94\tEvaluating cheapest-first optimizes work. Applying authority correctly prevents false positives from incidental path matches \u2014 a stray `manifests/` directory in a Go project does not make the project Kubernetes.\n95\t\n96\t### Plugin-root resolution failure\n97\t\n98\tIf every `Read` attempt in Algorithm step 1 fails \u2014 i.e., the plugin root could not be resolved (the variable is unset for the main agent, or no `## Plugin Root` path was provided to a sub-agent) or the paths do not exist \u2014 the procedure cannot continue.\n99\t\n100\tOn that failure:\n101\t\n102\t1. Emit an actionable error pointing to `CLAUDE.md` \u00a7Profile Conventions.\n103\t2. Return an empty result set so the calling skill falls back to generic guidance rather than panicking.\n104\t3. Do not retry; do not silently guess a path.\n105\t\n106\tConsumers inherit this check by invoking the shared procedure \u2014 no skill re-implements it.\n107\t\n108\t### Output shape\n109\t\n110\tA list of records, one per matched profile:\n111\t\n112\t```\n113\t[\n114\t  {\n115\t    profile: \"<name>\",                     // directory name under profiles/\n116\t    triggered_by: [\n117\t      \"filename: Chart.yaml\",              // signal type + matched value\n118\t      \"content: apiVersion+kind in block 2\"\n119\t    ],\n120\t    files: [\n121\t      \"path/to/file1.yaml\",\n122\t      \"path/to/file2.yaml\"\n123\t    ]\n124\t  },\n125\t  ...\n126\t]\n127\t```\n128\t\n129\tField semantics:\n130\t\n131\t- `profile` \u2014 the directory name under `profiles/` (e.g., `go`, `python`, `k8s`).\n132\t  Used downstream to resolve `profiles/<profile>/<phase>/index.md`,\n133\t  where `<phase>` is the profile phase subdirectory named identically to the calling skill:\n134\t  `review-code/`, `review-spec/`, `design/`, `implement/`, `test/`, or `document/`.\n135\t- `triggered_by` \u2014 which signal type fired and the specific value that matched.\n136\t  For debugging and for explaining detection to the user; never used as the key for profile lookup.\n137\t- `files` \u2014 the subset of input files that activated this profile.\n138\t  Skills use this to scope behavior (e.g., `helm lint` runs only on files triggered under Helm filename signals, not on every YAML in the diff).\n139\t\n140\tWhen no profile matches, return the empty list `[]`. The caller falls back to generic guidance, identical to today's \"no language detected\" path.\n141\t"
  }
}

    "type": "tool_result",
    "content": "1\t## Review scope protocol\n2\t\n3\tIsolated reviewers see the diff and the design docs but have zero visibility into which tasks are done versus pending. Without scope, they flag code-that-isn't-written-yet as `MISSING_IMPL` (spec review) or as missing functionality (code review). This protocol defines a scope artifact both skills inject into their sub-agent and external-reviewer prompts.\n4\t\n5\t### Input shapes\n6\t\n7\tThe review workflow must handle three invocation shapes:\n8\t\n9\t1. **Invoked from `/kk:implement`** \u2014 `/docs/feat/wip/[feature]/` exists with `design.md`, `implementation.md`, `tasks.md`. The current task id is known (the one just finished). Use full scope filtering.\n10\t2. **Invoked directly inside a feature** \u2014 `/docs/feat/wip/[feature]/` exists but no current task is explicitly supplied. Derive scope from `tasks.md` status fields (`done` = in scope, `pending`/`in-progress` = out of scope).\n11\t3. **Invoked on an arbitrary diff** \u2014 no `/docs/feat/wip/` directory relates to the diff. Skip the scope artifact entirely and note \"No task scope available\" in the reviewer prompt.\n12\t\n13\t### Scope artifact format\n14\t\n15\tWhen shape 1 or 2 applies, build this block and inject it into every reviewer prompt (sub-agent prompt template and the external-reviewer `step` framing):\n16\t\n17\t```\n18\t## Task Scope\n19\t\n20\tReview mode: {mid-implementation | post-implementation}\n21\t\n22\tIn scope (review these):\n23\t- Task {id}: {title} \u2014 status: done\n24\t- [...more done tasks if applicable]\n25\t\n26\tOut of scope (pending \u2014 DO NOT flag as missing):\n27\t- Task {id}: {title} \u2014 status: {pending|in-progress}\n28\t- [...more pending tasks]\n29\t\n30\tThe feature's design docs describe the full end state. Pending tasks are expected gaps in the current diff \u2014 treat their absence from the code as intentional, not as a finding. You may still flag issues within the in-scope tasks even if they reference pending tasks (e.g., a broken interface contract).\n31\t```\n32\t\n33\tWhen shape 3 applies, use:\n34\t\n35\t```\n36\t## Task Scope\n37\t\n38\tNo task scope available \u2014 review the diff on its own merits without assuming any feature-level design doc.\n39\t```\n40\t\n41\t### Determining mode\n42\t\n43\t- **mid-implementation** \u2014 at least one task in `tasks.md` has status `pending` or `in-progress`.\n44\t- **post-implementation** \u2014 all tasks are `done`.\n45\t\n46\tIn shape 1, the invoking workflow (`/kk:implement`) may pass the current task id directly; that task is automatically in scope even if `tasks.md` hasn't been updated yet.\n47\t\n48\t### Interpretation guidance for reviewers\n49\t\n50\tThe reviewer prompt must also carry this instruction (agent definitions should state it; the workflow relays it implicitly via the scope block):\n51\t\n52\t- Do **not** report missing-implementation findings for items covered only by out-of-scope tasks.\n53\t- Do still report real issues *within* the in-scope tasks, including ones that affect future pending work (e.g., a public API the pending task will depend on is shaped wrongly).\n54\t- If a finding would be valid *only* after pending work lands, mention it under \"Areas Not Covered\" instead of the P0\u2013P3 sections.\n55\t"
  }

    "content": "1\t## pal codereview invocation\n2\t\n3\t`pal codereview` is a **multi-turn** tool. Step 1 outlines the review strategy; the expert analysis that produces actual findings runs as a follow-up. A single-step call returns zero findings.\n4\t\n5\t### Step 1 \u2014 initial call\n6\t\n7\tUse these parameters:\n8\t\n9\t| Parameter | Value |\n10\t|---|---|\n11\t| `model` | The most capable model from `pal listmodels` |\n12\t| `step` | Framing instruction + task scope + spec context summary (see \u00a7`step` content below). Keep it lean \u2014 file contents belong in `relevant_files`, not here |\n13\t| `step_number` | `1` |\n14\t| `total_steps` | `2` |\n15\t| `next_step_required` | `true` |\n16\t| `review_validation_type` | `\"external\"` (enables expert follow-up that produces findings) |\n17\t| `thinking_mode` | `\"max\"` |\n18\t| `review_type` | `\"full\"` |\n19\t| `findings` | `\"Initial submission for review. No findings yet.\"` |\n20\t| `relevant_files` | Absolute paths \u2014 see \u00a7Assembling `relevant_files` below |\n21\t| `confidence` | `\"exploring\"` |\n22\t\n23\t#### `step` content\n24\t\n25\tThe `step` field carries the review framing \u2014 not the diff or file contents. Structure it as:\n26\t\n27\t1. **Framing instruction** \u2014 what kind of review this is and what to focus on.\n28\t2. **Task scope block** \u2014 in-scope vs out-of-scope tasks (prevents false positives on pending work).\n29\t3. **Spec context summary** \u2014 one-paragraph design intent if available (\"No spec context available\" otherwise).\n30\t4. **File manifest** \u2014 a categorized list of the paths in `relevant_files`, explaining each file's role. Paths only, no contents. Example:\n31\t\n32\t   ```\n33\t   Files provided via relevant_files:\n34\t   - Diff: /tmp/kk-review-code-a1b2c3d4.patch\n35\t   - Changed: src/service.go, src/handler.go\n36\t   - Surrounding: src/types.go, src/middleware.go\n37\t   - Review checklists: .../profiles/go/review-code/solid-checklist.md\n38\t   - Design: docs/feat/wip/auth-refactor/design.md, implementation.md\n39\t   ```\n40\t\n41\t   This tells pal which files are code under review, which are review criteria to apply, and which provide design intent. Without it, pal may treat checklists as code to review rather than guidance to follow.\n42\t\n43\tDo NOT inline file contents into `step`. These are passed via `relevant_files` \u2014 pal reads them with proper token budgeting and deduplication.\n44\t\n45\t#### Assembling `relevant_files`\n46\t\n47\tThe caller assembles this list from artifacts gathered during preparation. All paths must be absolute. Categories, in order:\n48\t\n49\t1. **Diff file** \u2014 the git diff written to a temp file via `mktemp` (e.g., `/tmp/kk-review-code-XXXXXXXX.patch`). The caller writes this file; pal reads it. Clean up after the review completes.\n50\t2. **Changed source files** \u2014 every file touched by the diff. These give pal the full file context around each change.\n51\t3. **Surrounding code files** \u2014 direct imports, callers (one level up), and adjacent same-package files that share types with the changed code. Capped at 10 files; prioritize imports and callers over adjacency. These enable cross-file reasoning (e.g., verifying a called function's signature, checking convention consistency).\n52\t4. **Profile checklist files** \u2014 resolved `(profile, checklist)` file paths from profile detection (e.g., `${TOOLBOX_PLUGIN_ROOT}/profiles/go/review-code/solid-checklist.md`). These give pal the same domain-specific review criteria as the sub-agent reviewer.\n53\t5. **Design/implementation docs** \u2014 `design.md` and `implementation.md` from the feature's `docs/feat/wip/<feature>/` directory, when available. These enable pal to flag spec deviations, not just code smells.\n54\t\n55\tThe caller is responsible for collecting these paths during its preparation steps and passing the assembled list here. pal handles file reading, token budgeting, and cross-turn deduplication internally.\n56\t\n57\t### Step 2 \u2014 continuation call\n58\t\n59\tAfter step 1 returns, make a follow-up call using the `continuation_id` from the step 1 response:\n60\t\n61\t| Parameter | Value |\n62\t|---|---|\n63\t| `model` | Same model as step 1 |\n64\t| `continuation_id` | From step 1 response |\n65\t| `step` | `\"Produce the expert analysis and final findings based on the review in step 1.\"` |\n66\t| `step_number` | `2` |\n67\t| `total_steps` | `2` |\n68\t| `next_step_required` | `false` |\n69\t| `findings` | Copy `findings` from step 1 response (or summarize if too large) |\n70\t| `confidence` | `\"high\"` |\n71\t\n72\t### Parallel execution with sub-agents\n73\t\n74\tThe step 1 call can be issued in the same message as the sub-agent (Agent tool) call \u2014 they execute in parallel. When both return, make the pal step 2 continuation call. The sub-agent typically takes longer than pal step 1, so the continuation call adds minimal wall-clock time.\n75\t\n76\t### Failure modes\n77\t\n78\t- `listmodels` returns no models \u2192 skip pal, proceed with sub-agent findings only\n79\t- Step 1 succeeds but step 2 fails \u2192 use any findings from step 1 response\n80\t- Both steps return zero issues \u2192 treat as a soft failure (pal produced no signal); note in the report and proceed with sub-agent findings\n81\t\n82\t`pal` is an external model with no conversation context \u2014 naturally isolated. Its output stays in **native format** \u2014 do NOT map it to the skill's finding types or severity levels.\n83\t"
  }
}

EVENT e48.0

  "id": "e48.0",
  "source_sequence": 48,

    "type": "tool_result",
    "content": "1\t### Workflow\n2\t\n3\t**Read [SKILL.md \u00a7Mandatory ordering \u2014 methodology before evidence](./SKILL.md#mandatory-ordering--methodology-before-evidence) before executing this file.** The steps below are strictly sequential. Do not read diff content, re-read changed files, or run `capy_search` before Step 5. Until then, `git diff --stat` (filenames only) is the only contact you have with the changes.\n4\t\n5\tCopy this checklist and check off items as you complete them:\n6\t\n7\t```\n8\tCode Review Progress:\n9\t- [ ] Step 1: Scope (filenames only)\n10\t- [ ] Step 2: Detect active profiles\n11\t- [ ] Step 3: Load profile review indexes (filename-evaluable entries)\n12\t- [ ] Step 4: Read resolved checklists\n13\t- [ ] Step 5: Read diff + re-read changed files + capy search\n14\t- [ ] Step 6: Resolve content-evaluable conditional entries\n15\t- [ ] Step 7: Apply checklists\n16\t- [ ] Step 8: Self-check and confidence assessment\n17\t- [ ] Step 9: Index findings\n18\t- [ ] Step 10: Present results\n19\t- [ ] Step 11: Next steps confirmation\n20\t- [ ] Step 12: Verify outputs\n21\t```\n22\t\n23\t---\n24\t\n25\t### 1) Scope (filenames only)\n26\t\n27\tRun `git status -sb` and `git diff --stat` to get the list of touched files. **Do not run `git diff` yet** \u2014 the full diff enters context in Step 5, after methodology is loaded.\n28\t\n29\t**Edge cases:**\n30\t\n31\t- **No changes**: If `git diff --stat` is empty, inform user and ask if they want to review staged changes or a specific commit range.\n32\t- **Large diff (>500 lines)**: Proceed through Steps 2\u20134 normally; Step 5 covers batching.\n33\t- **Mixed concerns**: Note the spread for Step 9 output; grouping happens at findings-emit time.\n34\t\n35\t### 2) Detect active profiles\n36\t\n37\tDelegate to [shared-profile-detection.md](shared-profile-detection.md). Input: the filename list from Step 1. The shared procedure iterates its own \u00a7Known profiles list and reads each profile's `DETECTION.md` via the `Read` tool \u2014 no filesystem enumeration, no `Glob`.\n38\t\n39\tThe shared procedure returns a list of records:\n40\t\n41\t```\n42\t[{ profile: \"<name>\", triggered_by: [...], files: [...] }, ...]\n43\t```\n44\t\n45\tHold this list for Step 3. There is no single \"primary language\"; any number of profiles can be active on the same diff (e.g., `go` + `k8s` when a Go service ships a Helm chart).\n46\t\n47\tIf the list is empty (no profile matched), skip Steps 3\u20134's profile-specific loading and proceed to Step 5 with general guidance only.\n48\t\n49\t### 3) Load profile review indexes\n50\t\n51\tFor each active profile record from Step 2:\n52\t\n53\t1. Read `${TOOLBOX_PLUGIN_ROOT}/profiles/<profile>/review-code/index.md`.\n54\t2. Collect every entry under **Always load**.\n55\t3. For every conditional entry (**Load if:** predicate), classify the predicate:\n56\t   - **Filename-evaluable** \u2014 the predicate is satisfied by filenames, extensions, or directory names alone (e.g., \"diff contains `Chart.yaml`\", \"file under `bases/` or `overlays/`\"). Evaluate now against the filename list from Step 1. If it matches, collect the entry into the `(profile, checklist)` list.\n57\t   - **Content-evaluable** \u2014 the predicate requires inspecting file bytes (e.g., YAML `kind:` field values, `apiVersion:` keys, specific string anchors). Do **not** read file content here. Instead, append the entry to a **deferred list** keyed by `(profile, checklist, predicate)` \u2014 Step 6 resolves this list after Step 5 reads content.\n58\t4. Append the collected filename-evaluable entries to the flat `(profile, checklist)` list.\n59\t\n60\tDo NOT hardcode checklist names \u2014 the index is authoritative, and new profiles or new conditional entries take effect without edits to this file.\n61\t\n62\t### 4) Read resolved checklists\n63\t\n64\tFor each `(profile, checklist)` record from Step 3, use the `Read` tool on `${TOOLBOX_PLUGIN_ROOT}/profiles/<profile>/review-code/<checklist>`. Every checklist file enters context now, before any diff content does. The review that follows in Step 6 reads *through* these checklists; if they are not loaded, the review cannot happen.\n65\t\n66\tThis is the single load-bearing gate of the workflow. If a checklist read fails (file missing, path unresolved), stop and surface the error \u2014 do not proceed with partial methodology.\n67\t\n68\t### 5) Read the diff, re-read changed files, run capy search\n69\t\n70\tNow, with every checklist in context, read the content:\n71\t\n72\t- Run `git diff` (full output) to capture the changes.\n73\t- **Re-read every changed file** using the Read tool. Do NOT rely on file contents read earlier in the conversation \u2014 code may have changed since (e.g., fixes applied between reviews in the same session).\n74\t- If needed, use `rg` or `grep` to find related modules, usages, and contracts.\n75\t- Identify entry points, ownership boundaries, and critical paths (auth, payments, data writes, network).\n76\t- **Capy search:** Search `kk:review-findings` for prior findings in the same files/modules. For each programming-language profile active (from Step 2), search `kk:lang-idioms` for best practices. If `kk:lang-idioms` returns no results for a language, optionally use `capy_fetch_and_index` to fetch a canonical idioms resource (e.g., Effective Go for `go`) and label it `kk:lang-idioms`. Skip the lookup for non-language profiles (e.g., `k8s`) \u2014 `kk:lang-idioms` is a programming-language idiom store.\n77\t\n78\tThis is the only step that reads artifact content. It appears once, by design. Do not repeat `git diff` or file re-reads in later steps.\n79\t\n80\t### 6) Resolve content-evaluable conditional entries\n81\t\n82\tFor every `(profile, checklist, predicate)` record on the deferred list from Step 3:\n83\t\n84\t1. Evaluate the predicate against the file content now available from Step 5. Apply the same bounded-inspection rules as the shared profile-detection procedure \u2014 ~16 KB per file; multi-document YAML inspected per `---`-separated block.\n85\t2. If the predicate matches, use the `Read` tool on `${TOOLBOX_PLUGIN_ROOT}/profiles/<profile>/review-code/<checklist>` to load the checklist into context, then append `(profile, checklist)` to the flat list that Step 7 iterates.\n86\t3. If the predicate does not match, drop the entry silently \u2014 no checklist is loaded for it.\n87\t\n88\tIf the deferred list is empty (no profile contributed a content-evaluable conditional), this step is a no-op. Proceed to Step 7.\n89\t\n90\tThe deferred list exists so Step 3 can remain a filenames-only step (per the mandatory ordering in SKILL.md) while conditionals that genuinely require content \u2014 e.g., loading `reliability-checklist.md` on diffs that contain a `kind: Deployment` YAML document \u2014 still reach Step 7. Content-evaluable conditionals that bypass this step will be silently dropped.\n91\t\n92\t### 7) Apply checklists\n93\t\n94\tIterate the flat `(profile, checklist, triggered_by)` list \u2014 the union of Step 3's filename-evaluable entries (whose checklists were read in Step 4) and Step 6's content-evaluable entries (whose checklists were read in Step 6 itself). For each record, apply the checklist (already in context) to the diff (in context from Step 5). A checklist may cover SOLID/architecture, security, quality, removal, or a profile-specific concern (e.g., Helm template correctness, RBAC least privilege) \u2014 the checklist itself states what to look for.\n95\t\n96\tTag each finding with its `(profile, checklist)` origin and the `triggered_by` signal from Step 2's detection output. These materialize as per-finding sub-labels inside the severity-major template in Step 10 \u2014 not as separate profile-grouped sections. For generic findings (SOLID, security, code quality, removal) not sourced from a profile checklist, use `Profile: generic \u00b7 Checklist: \u2014` and `Triggered by: \u2014`.\n97\t\n98\tGeneral guidance that applies regardless of profile \u2014 apply these categories on every diff, whether or not a profile-specific checklist covered them:\n99\t\n100\t- **SOLID / architecture:** SRP violations (overloaded modules with unrelated responsibilities), OCP (frequent edits to add behavior instead of extension points), LSP (subclasses that break expectations or require type checks), ISP (wide interfaces with unused methods), DIP (high-level logic tied to low-level implementations). When you propose a refactor, explain _why_ it improves cohesion/coupling and outline a minimal, safe split. If refactor is non-trivial, propose an incremental plan instead of a large rewrite.\n101\t- **Security / reliability:** XSS, injection (SQL/NoSQL/command), SSRF, path traversal; AuthZ/AuthN gaps, missing tenancy checks; secret leakage or API keys in logs/env/files; rate limits, unbounded loops, CPU/memory hotspots; unsafe deserialization, weak crypto, insecure defaults; race conditions, check-then-act, TOCTOU, missing locks. Call out both **exploitability** and **impact**.\n102\t- **Code quality:** error handling (swallowed exceptions, overly broad catch, missing handling, async errors); performance (N+1 queries, CPU-intensive ops in hot paths, missing cache, unbounded memory); boundary conditions (null/undefined, empty collections, numeric boundaries, off-by-one). Flag issues that may cause silent failures or production incidents.\n103\t- **Removal candidates:** unused, redundant, or feature-flagged-off code. Distinguish **safe delete now** vs **defer with plan**; provide concrete follow-up steps with checkpoints (tests/metrics).\n104\t\n105\t### 8) Self-check and confidence assessment\n106\t\n107\tFor **each finding** from Step 7:\n108\t\n109\t1. Re-read the relevant code and surrounding context independently.\n110\t2. Ask: **\"Could I be misreading the code?\"** \u2014 trace execution paths, check for runtime behavior, configuration, or framework conventions that might make this correct.\n111\t3. Ask: **\"Is this a real issue or a style preference?\"** \u2014 distinguish between bugs/risks and subjective choices that don't affect correctness or security.\n112\t4. Ask: **\"What's the actual impact?\"** \u2014 verify that the severity matches the real-world consequence, not just the theoretical violation.\n113\t5. Assign final confidence score (1\u2013100%) with **explicit reasoning** documenting:\n114\t   - What was verified\n115\t   - What evidence supports the finding\n116\t   - What uncertainty remains\n117\t6. Downgrade or **remove** findings that don't survive the self-check.\n118\t\n119\t### 9) Index findings\n120\t\n121\tIndex any P0/P1 findings that suggest a systemic or structural pattern (not isolated typos or one-off mistakes) as `kk:review-findings`. Index on first encounter \u2014 recurrence detection happens on the search side in future reviews.\n122\t\n123\t- If no P0/P1 systemic findings exist, explicitly note \"No findings to index\" and move on.\n124\t- This step is mandatory \u2014 do not skip it even if the review found no issues.\n125\t\n126\t### 10) Present results\n127\t\n128\t#### Output format\n129\t\n130\tStructure your review as follows:\n131\t\n132\t```markdown\n133\t## Code Review Summary\n134\t\n135\t**Files reviewed**: X files, Y lines changed\n136\t**Overall assessment**: [APPROVE / REQUEST_CHANGES / COMMENT]\n137\t\n138\t---\n139\t\n140\t## Findings\n141\t\n142\t### P0 - Critical\n143\t\n144\t(none or list)\n145\t\n146\t### P1 - High\n147\t\n148\t- **[file:line]** Brief title\n149\t  - Profile: {profile_name} \u00b7 Checklist: {checklist_filename}\n150\t  - Triggered by: {signal_type} \u2014 {signal_description}\n151\t  - Description of issue\n152\t  - Confidence: 90% - reasoning behind the confidence level\n153\t  - Suggested fix\n154\t\n155\t- **[another_file:line]** Brief title\n156\t  - Profile: generic \u00b7 Checklist: \u2014\n157\t  - Triggered by: \u2014\n158\t  - Description of issue\n159\t  - Confidence: 60% - reasoning behind the confidence level\n160\t  - Suggested fix\n161\t\n162\t### P2 - Medium\n163\t\n164\t...\n165\t\n166\t### P3 - Low\n167\t\n168\t...\n169\t\n170\t---\n171\t\n172\t## Removal/Iteration Plan\n173\t\n174\t(if applicable)\n175\t\n176\t## Additional Suggestions\n177\t\n178\t(optional improvements, not blocking)\n179\t```\n180\t\n181\t**Inline comments**: Use this format for file-specific findings:\n182\t\n183\t```\n184\t::code-comment{file=\"path/to/file\" line=\"42\" severity=\"P1\"}\n185\tDescription of the issue and suggested fix.\n186\t::\n187\t```\n188\t\n189\t**Clean review**: If no issues found, explicitly state:\n190\t\n191\t- What was checked\n192\t- Any areas not covered (e.g., \"Did not verify database migrations\")\n193\t- Residual risks or recommended follow-up tests\n194\t\n195\t### 11) Next steps confirmation\n196\t\n197\tAfter presenting findings, ask user how to proceed:\n198\t\n199\t```markdown\n200\t---\n201\t\n202\t## Next Steps\n203\t\n204\tI found X issues (P0: ..., P1: ..., P2: ..., P3: ...).\n205\t\n206\tThe actionable items I recommend fixing:\n207\t1. P1 ...\n208\t2. P2 ...\n209\t3. P3 ...\n210\t\n211\tItems I recommend keeping as is:\n212\t- ...: the finding is a false-positive because ...\n213\t- ...: correct behavior because ...\n214\t- ...: mirrors production behavior of ...\n215\t\n216\t**How would you like to proceed?**\n217\t\n218\t1. **Fix all** - I'll implement all suggested fixes\n219\t2. **Fix P0/P1 only** - Address critical and high priority issues\n220\t3. **Fix specific items** - Tell me which issues to fix\n221\t4. **No changes** - Review complete, no implementation needed\n222\t\n223\tPlease choose an option or provide specific instructions.\n224\t```\n225\t\n226\t**Important**: Do NOT implement any changes until user explicitly confirms. This is a review-first workflow.\n227\t\n228\t### 12) Verify outputs\n229\t\n230\tBefore declaring the review complete, check each item in the **Required Outputs** section of SKILL.md:\n231\t\n232\t- [ ] Review report presented to user\n233\t- [ ] P0/P1 systemic findings indexed as `kk:review-findings` (or explicitly noted \"No findings to index\")\n234\t- [ ] Next steps confirmation from user\n235\t\n236\tIf any item is unchecked, go back and complete it before proceeding.\n237\t"
  }
}

    "content": "1\t# Kubernetes \u2014 detection\n2\t\n3\tDeclares when the `k8s` profile activates on a given set of files. Consumed by `klaude-plugin/skills/_shared/profile-detection.md`. Detection is additive: multiple profiles may activate on the same diff (e.g., `go` + `k8s`).\n4\t\n5\tEvaluation follows the shared cost-ordered procedure (path \u2192 filename \u2192 content). Authority runs filename \u2248 content > path: filename or content signals activate the profile; path alone never does, it only promotes a file to a candidate.\n6\t\n7\t## Path signals\n8\t\n9\tCase-insensitive substring match anywhere in the file's path. Pre-filter only \u2014 a path hit alone does NOT activate the profile.\n10\t\n11\t- `k8s/`\n12\t- `manifests/`\n13\t- `charts/`\n14\t- `kustomize/`\n15\t- `deploy/`\n16\t- `templates/`\n17\t\n18\t## Filename signals\n19\t\n20\tAuthoritative: any match activates the profile. Filename matches short-circuit content inspection for the matched file.\n21\t\n22\t- `Chart.yaml` (exact) \u2192 Helm chart root.\n23\t- Any filename starting with `values` (e.g., `values.yaml`, `values.yml`, `values-prod.yaml`, `values-prod-v2-final.yaml`) when the containing directory also contains `Chart.yaml` \u2192 Helm values by adjacency. The `values*` glob has no upper bound on the wildcard; the adjacency rule (sibling `Chart.yaml` in the same directory) is the binding constraint. The match is filename-plus-adjacency only \u2014 file content is not inspected, so a file named `values-backup.yaml` next to a `Chart.yaml` activates regardless of what it actually contains.\n24\t- Any file with extension `.yaml`, `.yml`, or `.tpl` inside `<dir>/templates/` where `<dir>` itself contains a `Chart.yaml` as a direct child \u2192 Helm template. The binding constraint is that the `templates/` directory must sit *directly* next to a `Chart.yaml` \u2014 i.e., at a chart root or a subchart root under `<parent>/charts/<subchart>/`. A `templates/` nested elsewhere in the tree (e.g., `docs/templates/`, `ci/templates/`) does NOT activate this rule even when a `Chart.yaml` sits at the repository root, because `docs/` and `ci/` do not themselves contain a `Chart.yaml`. This avoids the monorepo false-positive where a repo-root umbrella `Chart.yaml` would otherwise claim every `templates/` directory in the tree. It still avoids the trap where a standalone edit to `<chart-root>/templates/deployment.yaml` contains `{{ if ... }}` directives before any `apiVersion:` and would otherwise fail the content signal.\n25\t- Exact filenames `kustomization.yaml`, `kustomization.yml`, or `Kustomization` \u2192 Kustomize.\n26\t\n27\t## Content signals\n28\t\n29\tAuthoritative for generic YAML files (`.yaml` or `.yml`) not already caught by a filename signal. Inspection is bounded to the first ~16 KB per file; large generated manifests beyond that bound are not inspected.\n30\t\n31\t- Split the file on `---` document separators. For each `---`-separated document block, check for a top-level `apiVersion:` AND a top-level `kind:` \u2014 parsed as YAML mapping keys at zero indent, not as substrings inside block scalars (`|`, `>`) or comments. A block satisfying both is a Kubernetes manifest document.\n32\t- One matching document activates the profile for that file. The first document need not match \u2014 a file whose second or later document is the only K8s document still activates.\n33\t- A `.yaml` / `.yml` file with no matching document in any block \u2192 not Kubernetes. (It may still match another profile; generic YAML belongs to no profile by default.)\n34\t\n35\t---\n36\t\n37\t## Multi-profile behavior\n38\t\n39\tThe Kubernetes profile is **additive**. It coexists with programming-language profiles or any other IaC profile on the same diff. When Go source files sit alongside Kubernetes manifests, both `go` and `k8s` activate; downstream skills consult both profiles' content and emit findings grouped by `(profile, checklist)`.\n40\t\n41\t## Design signals\n42\t\n43\tdisplay_name: Kubernetes\n44\ttokens:\n45\t  - Kubernetes\n46\t  - K8s\n47\t  - Helm chart\n48\t  - kubectl\n49\t  - kustomize\n50\t  - manifest.yaml\n51\t  - Deployment resource\n52\t  - StatefulSet\n53\t  - DaemonSet\n54\t  - CronJob\n55\t\n56\t## Dockerfile non-trigger\n57\t\n58\tA Dockerfile on its own \u2014 even under a `deploy/` or `k8s/` directory \u2014 does NOT activate the `k8s` profile. Dockerfiles match no filename signal here (they are not `Chart.yaml` / `values*.yaml` / `kustomization.yaml`) and no content signal (they do not contain `apiVersion:` + `kind:`). When a Dockerfile appears in the same diff as Kubernetes manifests, `k8s` activates on the manifests' signals; the Dockerfile itself is not reviewed by this profile. A future container profile may own Dockerfiles independently.\n59\t"
  }

Inspection is bounded to the first ~16 KB per file.\n26\t\n27\t- A `Makefile` containing the literal string `controller-gen` OR a target named `manifests` (line starting with `manifests:`) \u2192 kubebuilder/operator-sdk generated Makefile with CRD/RBAC generation targets.\n28\t- A Go source file (`.go` extension) containing an import of `sigs.k8s.io/controller-runtime` \u2192 controller-runtime dependency, the standard library for writing Kubernetes controllers.\n29\t\n30\t## Design signals\n31\t\n32\tdisplay_name: Kubernetes Operator\n33\ttokens:\n34\t  - operator\n35\t  - controller\n36\t  - kubebuilder\n37\t  - controller-runtime\n38\t  - CRD authoring\n39\t  - custom resource definition authoring\n40\t  - reconciliation loop\n41\t"

    "content": "1\t# Security and Reliability Checklist\n2\t\n3\t## Contents\n4\t- Input/Output Safety (XSS, injection, SSRF, path traversal, unsafe deserialization)\n5\t- AuthN/AuthZ\n6\t- JWT & Token Security\n7\t- Secrets and PII\n8\t- Supply Chain & Dependencies\n9\t- CORS & Headers\n10\t- Runtime Risks (unbounded ops, GIL contention, resource exhaustion)\n11\t- Cryptography\n12\t- Race Conditions (shared state, TOCTOU, database concurrency, distributed)\n13\t- Data Integrity\n14\t\n15\t## Input/Output Safety\n16\t\n17\t- **XSS**: Unsafe HTML injection, Jinja2 `|safe` filter or `Markup()` bypassing auto-escaping, raw string interpolation in HTML responses\n18\t- **Injection**: SQL injection via f-strings or `%` formatting instead of parameterized queries; command injection via `os.system`, `subprocess.run(shell=True)` with user input\n19\t- **SSRF**: User-controlled URLs reaching internal services without allowlist validation\n20\t- **Path traversal**: User input in file paths without sanitization (`../` attacks), `os.path.join` with absolute user input bypassing base directory\n21\t- **Unsafe deserialization**: Using `pickle.loads`, `yaml.load` (without `SafeLoader`), or `eval`/`exec` on untrusted input\n22\t- **Code injection via imports**: User input reaching `__import__`, `importlib.import_module`, or dynamic module loading\n23\t\n24\t## AuthN/AuthZ\n25\t\n26\t- Missing tenant or ownership checks for read/write operations\n27\t- New endpoints without auth guards or RBAC enforcement\n28\t- Trusting client-provided roles/flags/IDs\n29\t- Broken access control (IDOR - Insecure Direct Object Reference)\n30\t- Session fixation or weak session management\n31\t\n32\t## JWT & Token Security\n33\t\n34\t- Algorithm confusion attacks (accepting `none` or `HS256` when expecting `RS256`)\n35\t- Weak or hardcoded secrets\n36\t- Missing expiration (`exp`) or not validating it\n37\t- Sensitive data in JWT payload (tokens are base64, not encrypted)\n38\t- Not validating `iss` (issuer) or `aud` (audience)\n39\t\n40\t## Secrets and PII\n41\t\n42\t- API keys, tokens, or credentials in code/config/logs\n43\t- Secrets in git history or environment variables exposed to client\n44\t- Excessive logging of PII or sensitive payloads\n45\t- Missing data masking in error messages\n46\t\n47\t## Supply Chain & Dependencies\n48\t\n49\t- Unpinned dependencies allowing malicious updates (missing version pins in `requirements.txt`/`pyproject.toml`)\n50\t- Dependency confusion (private package name collision on PyPI)\n51\t- Importing from untrusted sources without integrity checks\n52\t- Outdated dependencies with known CVEs\n53\t\n54\t## CORS & Headers\n55\t\n56\t- Overly permissive CORS (`Access-Control-Allow-Origin: *` with credentials)\n57\t- Missing security headers (CSP, X-Frame-Options, X-Content-Type-Options)\n58\t- Exposed internal headers or stack traces\n59\t\n60\t## Runtime Risks\n61\t\n62\t- Unbounded loops, recursive calls, or large in-memory buffers\n63\t- Missing timeouts, retries, or rate limiting on external calls\n64\t- Blocking operations on the event loop (sync I/O in `async` context)\n65\t- Resource exhaustion (file handles, connections, memory)\n66\t- **`assert` stripped in optimized mode**: `python -O` removes all `assert` statements \u2014 never use `assert` for security/input validation\n67\t- ReDoS (Regular Expression Denial of Service)\n68\t\n69\t## Cryptography\n70\t\n71\t- Weak algorithms (MD5, SHA1 for security purposes)\n72\t- Hardcoded IVs or salts\n73\t- Using encryption without authentication (ECB mode, no HMAC)\n74\t- Insufficient key length\n75\t- Using `==` for secret/token comparison instead of `hmac.compare_digest` (leaks timing information)\n76\t\n77\t## Race Conditions\n78\t\n79\tRace conditions are subtle bugs that cause intermittent failures and security vulnerabilities. Pay special attention to:\n80\t\n81\t### Shared State Access\n82\t- Multiple threads accessing shared variables without locks (GIL does not protect multi-step operations)\n83\t- Global state or module-level mutables modified concurrently\n84\t- Lazy initialization without proper locking\n85\t- Non-thread-safe collections used in concurrent context\n86\t\n87\t### Check-Then-Act (TOCTOU)\n88\t- `if (exists) then use` patterns without atomic operations\n89\t- `if (authorized) then perform` where authorization can change\n90\t- File existence check followed by file operation\n91\t- Balance check followed by deduction (financial operations)\n92\t- Inventory check followed by order placement\n93\t- `tempfile.mktemp()` is deprecated (TOCTOU race) \u2014 use `tempfile.mkstemp()` or `NamedTemporaryFile`\n94\t\n95\t### Database Concurrency\n96\t- Missing optimistic locking (`version` column, `updated_at` checks)\n97\t- Missing pessimistic locking (`SELECT FOR UPDATE`)\n98\t- Read-modify-write without transaction isolation\n99\t- Counter increments without atomic operations (`UPDATE SET count = count + 1`)\n100\t- Unique constraint violations in concurrent inserts\n101\t\n102\t### Distributed Systems\n103\t- Missing distributed locks for shared resources\n104\t- Leader election race conditions\n105\t- Cache invalidation races (stale reads after writes)\n106\t- Event ordering dependencies without proper sequencing\n107\t- Split-brain scenarios in cluster operations\n108\t\n109\t### Common Patterns to Flag\n110\t```python\n111\t# Dangerous patterns:\n112\t\n113\t# TOCTOU\n114\tif not os.path.exists(path):\n115\t    open(path, \"w\").write(data)\n116\t\n117\t# Read-modify-write\n118\tvalue = cache.get(key)\n119\tvalue += 1\n120\tcache.set(key, value)\n121\t\n122\t# Check-then-act\n123\tif user.balance >= amount:\n124\t    user.balance -= amount\n125\t    user.save()\n126\t```\n127\t\n128\t### Questions to Ask\n129\t- \"What happens if two requests hit this code simultaneously?\"\n130\t- \"Is this operation atomic or can it be interrupted?\"\n131\t- \"What shared state does this code access?\"\n132\t- \"How does this behave under high concurrency?\"\n133\t\n134\t## Data Integrity\n135\t\n136\t- Missing transactions, partial writes, or inconsistent state updates\n137\t- Weak validation before persistence (implicit type conversion issues)\n138\t- Missing idempotency for retryable operations\n139\t- Lost updates due to concurrent modifications\n140\t"
  }
}

EVENT e102.0
{
  "id": "e102.0",
  "source_sequence": 102,
  "session_id": "bcb47d65-8609-4412-b2b2-ea3dba438aa6",

    "type": "tool_result",
    "content": "1\t# SOLID Smell Prompts\n2\t\n3\t## SRP (Single Responsibility)\n4\t\n5\t- File owns unrelated concerns (e.g., HTTP + DB + domain rules in one file)\n6\t- Large class/module with low cohesion or multiple reasons to change\n7\t- Functions that orchestrate many unrelated steps\n8\t- God objects that know too much about the system\n9\t- Circular imports between modules (signals tangled responsibilities \u2014 causes `ImportError`/`AttributeError` at runtime)\n10\t- **Ask**: \"What is the single reason this module would change?\"\n11\t\n12\t## OCP (Open/Closed)\n13\t\n14\t- Adding a new behavior requires editing many `if/elif` chains (or `match/case` blocks)\n15\t- Feature growth requires modifying core logic rather than extending\n16\t- No plugin/strategy/hook points for variation\n17\t- **Ask**: \"Can I add a new variant without touching existing code?\"\n18\t\n19\t## LSP (Liskov Substitution)\n20\t\n21\t- Subclass checks for concrete type via `isinstance` or raises for base method\n22\t- Overridden methods weaken preconditions or strengthen postconditions\n23\t- Subclass ignores or no-ops parent behavior\n24\t- **Ask**: \"Can I substitute any subclass without the caller knowing?\"\n25\t\n26\t## ISP (Interface Segregation)\n27\t\n28\t- ABCs/Protocols with many methods, most unused by implementers\n29\t- Callers depend on broad interfaces for narrow needs\n30\t- `NotImplementedError` stubs in subclasses\n31\t- **Ask**: \"Do all implementers use all methods?\"\n32\t\n33\t## DIP (Dependency Inversion)\n34\t\n35\t- High-level logic depends on concrete IO, storage, or network types\n36\t- Hard-coded implementations instead of abstractions or injection\n37\t- Import chains that couple business logic to infrastructure\n38\t- **Ask**: \"Can I swap the implementation without changing business logic?\"\n39\t\n40\t---\n41\t\n42\t## Common Code Smells (Beyond SOLID)\n43\t\n44\t| Smell | Signs |\n45\t|-------|-------|\n46\t| **Long method** | Function > 30 lines, multiple levels of nesting |\n47\t| **Feature envy** | Method uses more data from another class than its own |\n48\t| **Data clumps** | Same group of parameters passed together repeatedly |\n49\t| **Primitive obsession** | Using strings/numbers instead of domain types |\n50\t| **Shotgun surgery** | One change requires edits across many files |\n51\t| **Divergent change** | One file changes for many unrelated reasons |\n52\t| **Dead code** | Unreachable or never-called code |\n53\t| **Speculative generality** | Abstractions for hypothetical future needs |\n54\t| **Magic numbers/strings** | Hardcoded values without named constants |\n55\t| **Expensive `@property`** | Property getter performs I/O, DB queries, or heavy computation \u2014 callers expect attribute access to be cheap |\n56\t\n57\t---\n58\t\n59\t## Refactor Heuristics\n60\t\n61\t1. **Split by responsibility, not by size** - A small file can still violate SRP\n62\t2. **Introduce abstraction only when needed** - Wait for the second use case\n63\t3. **Keep refactors incremental** - Isolate behavior before moving\n64\t4. **Preserve behavior first** - Add tests before restructuring\n65\t5. **Name things by intent** - If naming is hard, the abstraction might be wrong\n66\t6. **Prefer composition over inheritance** - Multiple inheritance creates MRO complexity and tight coupling\n67\t7. **Make illegal states unrepresentable** - Use types to enforce invariants\n68\t"
  }
}

    "content": "1\t# Code Quality Checklist\n2\t\n3\t## Contents\n4\t- Error Handling (anti-patterns, best practices, questions)\n5\t- Performance & Caching (CPU, database/IO, caching, memory)\n6\t- Boundary Conditions (None handling, empty collections, numeric, string)\n7\t\n8\t## Error Handling\n9\t\n10\t### Anti-patterns to Flag\n11\t\n12\t- **Swallowed exceptions**: Empty `except` blocks or catch with only logging. Example:\n13\t  ```python\n14\t  try: ...\n15\t  except Exception: pass              # Silent failure\n16\t  try: ...\n17\t  except Exception as e: print(e)     # Log and forget, no re-raise\n18\t  ```\n19\t- **Overly broad except**: Catching bare `Exception` or `BaseException` instead of specific types\n20\t- **Error information leakage**: Stack traces or internal details exposed to users\n21\t- **Missing error handling**: No try-except around fallible operations (I/O, network, parsing)\n22\t- **Async error handling**: Unhandled exceptions in `asyncio` tasks, missing `try/except` in coroutines, fire-and-forget tasks without error callbacks\n23\t\n24\t### Best Practices to Check\n25\t\n26\t- [ ] Errors are caught at appropriate boundaries\n27\t- [ ] Error messages are user-friendly (no internal details exposed)\n28\t- [ ] Errors are logged with sufficient context for debugging\n29\t- [ ] Async task exceptions are retrieved or handled (not silently dropped)\n30\t- [ ] Fallback behavior is defined for recoverable errors\n31\t- [ ] Critical errors trigger alerts/monitoring\n32\t\n33\t### Questions to Ask\n34\t- \"What happens when this operation fails?\"\n35\t- \"Will the caller know something went wrong?\"\n36\t- \"Is there enough context to debug this error?\"\n37\t\n38\t---\n39\t\n40\t## Performance & Caching\n41\t\n42\t### CPU-Intensive Operations\n43\t\n44\t- **Expensive operations in hot paths**: `re.compile` in loops, JSON parsing in hot paths, crypto in loops\n45\t- **Blocking the event loop**: Sync I/O or heavy computation in `async` functions without `run_in_executor`\n46\t- **Unnecessary recomputation**: Same calculation done multiple times\n47\t- **Missing caching**: Pure functions called repeatedly with same inputs, no `@functools.lru_cache` or `@functools.cache`\n48\t- **GIL limitation for CPU-bound work**: `threading` does not parallelize CPU-bound tasks due to the GIL \u2014 use `multiprocessing` or `concurrent.futures.ProcessPoolExecutor`\n49\t\n50\t### Database & I/O\n51\t\n52\t- **N+1 queries**: Loop that makes a query per item instead of batch\n53\t  ```python\n54\t  # Bad: N+1\n55\t  for id in ids:\n56\t      user = db.execute(\"SELECT * FROM users WHERE id = %s\", (id,)).fetchone()\n57\t\n58\t  # Good: Batch\n59\t  users = db.execute(\"SELECT * FROM users WHERE id IN %s\", (tuple(ids),)).fetchall()\n60\t  ```\n61\t- **Missing indexes**: Queries on unindexed columns\n62\t- **Over-fetching**: SELECT * when only few columns needed\n63\t- **No pagination**: Loading entire dataset into memory\n64\t\n65\t### Caching Issues\n66\t\n67\t- **Missing cache for expensive operations**: Repeated API calls, DB queries, computations\n68\t- **Cache without TTL**: Stale data served indefinitely\n69\t- **Cache without invalidation strategy**: Data updated but cache not cleared\n70\t- **Cache key collisions**: Insufficient key uniqueness\n71\t- **Caching user-specific data globally**: Security/privacy issue\n72\t\n73\t### Memory\n74\t\n75\t- **Unbounded collections**: Lists/dicts that grow without limit\n76\t- **Large object retention**: Holding references preventing GC, circular references\n77\t- **String concatenation in loops**: Use `\"\".join(parts)` instead\n78\t- **Loading large files entirely**: Use streaming/iterator patterns instead\n79\t- **Mutable default arguments**: `def foo(items=[])` \u2014 default list is shared across all calls, mutating it causes cross-call contamination\n80\t- **Generator exhaustion**: Iterating a generator/iterator twice silently produces no results on the second pass\n81\t- **Missing context managers**: Not using `with` for files, DB connections, locks \u2014 resources leak on exceptions\n82\t- **Late binding closures**: Lambdas/closures in loops capture the variable by reference, not by value \u2014 all closures see the final loop value\n83\t- **`__del__` unreliability**: `__del__` is not guaranteed to run \u2014 never rely on it for cleanup; use context managers or `atexit`\n84\t\n85\t### Questions to Ask\n86\t- \"What's the time complexity of this operation?\"\n87\t- \"How does this behave with 10x/100x data?\"\n88\t- \"Is this result cacheable? Should it be?\"\n89\t- \"Can this be batched instead of one-by-one?\"\n90\t\n91\t---\n92\t\n93\t## Boundary Conditions\n94\t\n95\t### None Handling\n96\t\n97\t- **Missing None checks**: Accessing attributes on potentially `None` objects (causes `AttributeError`)\n98\t- **Truthiness confusion**: `if value:` when `0`, `\"\"`, `[]`, `{}` are valid values\n99\t- **Excessive None checks**: Deep chains of `if x is not None and x.y is not None` hiding structural issues\n100\t- **`is` vs `==` confusion**: Using `is` to compare values instead of `==` \u2014 works unreliably due to interning (e.g., `x is \"hello\"` may pass for small strings but fail for others)\n101\t- **None vs missing inconsistency**: Mixed usage of `None` and sentinel values without clear convention\n102\t\n103\t### Empty Collections\n104\t\n105\t- **Empty list not handled**: Code assumes list has items\n106\t- **Empty dict edge case**: Iteration or key access on empty dict\n107\t- **First/last element access**: `items[0]` or `items[-1]` without length check\n108\t\n109\t### Numeric Boundaries\n110\t\n111\t- **Division by zero**: Missing check before division (raises `ZeroDivisionError`)\n112\t- **Integer overflow**: Python handles big ints natively, but watch for C-extension or struct boundaries\n113\t- **Floating point comparison**: Using `==` instead of `math.isclose`\n114\t- **Negative values**: Index or count that shouldn't be negative\n115\t- **Off-by-one errors**: Loop bounds, slicing, pagination\n116\t\n117\t### String Boundaries\n118\t\n119\t- **Empty string**: Not handled as edge case\n120\t- **Whitespace-only string**: Passes truthiness check but is effectively empty\n121\t- **Very long strings**: No length limits causing memory/display issues\n122\t- **Unicode edge cases**: Emoji, RTL text, combining characters\n123\t\n124\t### Common Patterns to Flag\n125\t\n126\t```python\n127\t# Dangerous: no None check (AttributeError if user or profile is None)\n128\tname = user.profile.name\n129\t\n130\t# Dangerous: list access without check (IndexError if items is empty)\n131\tfirst = items[0]\n132\t\n133\t# Dangerous: division without check (ZeroDivisionError if count is 0)\n134\tavg = total / count\n135\t\n136\t# Dangerous: truthiness check excludes valid values\n137\tif value:  ...   # fails for 0, \"\", [], {}, False\n138\t```\n139\t\n140\t### Questions to Ask\n141\t- \"What if this is None?\"\n142\t- \"What if this collection is empty?\"\n143\t- \"What's the valid range for this number?\"\n144\t- \"What happens at the boundaries (0, -1, sys.maxsize)?\"\n145\t"
  }
}
