    "content": "1\t---\n2\tname: review-code\n3\tdescription: |\n4\t  Code review of current git changes with an expert senior-engineer lens. Detects SOLID violations, security risks, and proposes actionable improvements.\n5\t  Use when performing code reviews.\n6\t---\n7\t\n8\t# Code Review\n9\t\n10\t## Overview\n11\t\n12\tReview whether the current changes achieve their intended behavior, preserve affected contracts and meet applicable delivery constraints. Apply SOLID, architecture, security, quality and removal guidance alongside that reasoning. Default to review-only output unless the user asks to implement changes.\n13\t\n14\t## Conventions\n15\t\n16\tLoad these through [packet-preparation.md](packet-preparation.md)'s preparation\n17\tsequence. It supplies complete original instruction text in fewer Read calls;\n18\tthe direct-read fallback uses the same sources.\n19\t\n20\t- **Read capy knowledge base conventions** at [shared-capy-knowledge-protocol.md](shared-capy-knowledge-protocol.md).\n21\t- **Always read change context** at [shared-change-context.md](shared-change-context.md) and the common method at [functional-review.md](functional-review.md), even when no profile matches.\n22\t- **Read task scope** at [shared-review-scope-protocol.md](shared-review-scope-protocol.md); pending work does not excuse regressions in current flows.\n23\t- **Read profile detection** at [shared-profile-detection.md](shared-profile-detection.md). Use its records to select and load domain guidance before investigation.\n24\t\n25\t## Required Outputs\n26\t\n27\tBefore declaring the review complete, verify all outputs are delivered:\n28\t\n29\t- [ ] Review report presented to user\n30\t- [ ] Intent, scope/baselines, inspected behavior, applicable compatibility conclusions and evidence limits reported\n31\t- [ ] P0/P1 systemic findings indexed as `kk:review-findings` (skip if no qualifying findings)\n32\t- [ ] Required next steps handled, or clean review closed without proposing new work\n33\t\n34\tIndexing is owned by this skill \u2014 callers (e.g., `/kk:implement`) do NOT duplicate it.\n35\t\n36\t## Review Modes\n37\t\n38\t### Standard Mode (`/kk:review-code`)\n39\t\n40\tReviews code in the main conversation context. Fast, single-pass review using the workflow below.\n41\t\n42\t### Isolated Mode (`/kk:review-code:isolated`)\n43\t\n44\tDelegates detection to independent reviewers that did not write the code, then annotates their findings with author context. Two parallel reviewers: a `code-reviewer` sub-agent and `pal codereview` (external model in native format). Produces a report organized by agreement level with corroborated findings highlighted.\n45\t\n46\t- **Cost**: Higher (sub-agent + external model + annotation)\n47\t- **Isolation**: True \u2014 reviewers have zero authorship bias or session context\n48\t- **Degradation**: Graceful \u2014 if one reviewer fails, proceeds with the other; if both fail, suggests standard mode fallback\n49\t- **Best for**: When extra rigor is worth the cost (pre-merge, high-stakes changes)\n50\t\n51\tSee [review-isolated.md](./review-isolated.md) for the isolated workflow.\n52\t\n53\t## Severity Levels\n54\t\n55\t| Level  | Name     | Description                                                      | Action                             |\n56\t| ------ | -------- | ---------------------------------------------------------------- | ---------------------------------- |\n57\t| **P0** | Critical | Demonstrated critical security, integrity or availability impact needing immediate mitigation | Must block merge                   |\n58\t| **P1** | High     | Logic error, significant SOLID violation, performance regression | Should fix before merge            |\n59\t| **P2** | Medium   | Code smell, maintainability concern, minor SOLID violation       | Fix in this PR or create follow-up |\n60\t| **P3** | Low      | Style, naming, minor suggestion                                  | Optional improvement               |\n61\t\n62\t## Workflow\n63\t\n64\t### Mandatory ordering \u2014 methodology before evidence\n65\t\n66\tThe standard workflow below is strictly sequential. **Load the process, shared protocols, common functional method and every resolved profile checklist before source investigation, `capy_search`, findings, edits or tests.** After basic process instructions load, the sole early-content exception is bounded inspection to resolve declared detection/conditional-load predicates: at most approximately 16 KiB per candidate file; log predicate/path, and conservatively load an instruction when its conditional is undecidable within the bound. No behavioral analysis or full-diff investigation is permitted during routing.\n67\t\n68\tThis ordering is load-bearing, not stylistic. Reviewing from a diff before loading profile checklists is the known failure mode this skill is designed to prevent: the LLM has enough from the diff to produce plausible findings, and optimizes away the methodology if the workflow permits.\n69\t\n70\t**Phases** (same order as [review-process.md](./review-process.md)):\n71\t\n72\t1. Prepare instruction packets \u2014 read [packet-preparation.md](packet-preparation.md), run the helper below for `bootstrap`, and Read every returned part; this loads process, shared protocols, common method and all known detection rules\n73\t2. Select the diff \u2014 use status/filenames only; task scope belongs to investigation\n74\t3. Detect profiles and route checklists \u2014 read and reconcile every known detection rule; filename/metadata signals, then bounded declared predicates; no deferred conditionals\n75\t4. Read every resolved checklist \u2014 build and Read the selected checklist packet; its manifest rejects missing index decisions and skipped always-load files. Confirm actual complete Read results before proceeding\n76\t5. Investigate \u2014 read available component contracts and establish context/task scope, then selected diff/current files; trace behavior and historical compatibility, and search relevant knowledge\n77\t6. Apply profile and general checklists to that behavior\n78\t7. Self-check findings and confidence through targeted verification\n79\t8. Index systemic P0/P1 findings as `kk:review-findings`\n80\t9. Present findings, coverage and evidence-qualified verdict\n81\t10. Close a clean review; confirm only supported next steps needing authorization\n82\t11. Verify required outputs\n83\t\n84\tSee [review-process.md](./review-process.md) for the detailed step-by-step process.\n85\t\n86\t**Preparation helper:** execute [scripts/prepare_instructions.py](scripts/prepare_instructions.py)\n87\twith Python 3 using its resolved path. The installed path is\n88\t`${CLAUDE_PLUGIN_ROOT}/skills/review-code/scripts/prepare_instructions.py`.\n89\tThe helper reads instructions only and prints packet paths. Creating a packet\n90\tdoes not load it: use Read on every part, including continuations, before source\n91\tinvestigation. If execution is unavailable, use the direct-read fallback.\n92\t\n93\t## Invocation\n94\t\n95\tStandard mode:\n96\t\n97\t```\n98\t/kk:review-code\n99\t```\n100\t\n101\tIsolated mode with independent sub-agents:\n102\t\n103\t```\n104\t/kk:review-code:isolated\n105\t```\n106\t"

Load basic instructions and every resolved profile checklist before investigating source, searching knowledge, forming findings or preparing evidence. The sole early-content exception is bounded routing for declared detection/conditional-load predicates: approximately 16 KiB per candidate file, predicate/path logged, conservative loading when undecidable. Routing permits no behavioral analysis, full-diff investigation, edits or tests.\n4\t\n5\t```\n6\tIsolated Code Review Progress:\n7\t- [ ] Step 1: Load basic instructions\n8\t- [ ] Step 2: Resolve scope from filenames/metadata only\n9\t- [ ] Step 3: Detect profiles and route all checklists\n10\t- [ ] Step 4: Read every checklist and confirm the loading gate\n11\t- [ ] Step 5: Investigate and prepare equivalent evidence for both reviewers\n12\t- [ ] Step 6: Spawn independent reviewers and resolve evidence requests\n13\t- [ ] Step 7: Annotate findings without overriding reviewers\n14\t- [ ] Step 8: Index systemic findings\n15\t- [ ] Step 9: Present findings, coverage and qualified verdict; confirm next steps\n16\t- [ ] Step 10: Verify outputs and clean up temporary evidence\n17\t```\n18\t\n19\t## Step 1: Load Basic Instructions\n20\t\n21\tRead [SKILL.md](SKILL.md), this workflow, [shared-capy-knowledge-protocol.md](shared-capy-knowledge-protocol.md), [shared-profile-detection.md](shared-profile-detection.md), [shared-review-scope-protocol.md](shared-review-scope-protocol.md), [shared-change-context.md](shared-change-context.md), [functional-review.md](functional-review.md), and [shared-pal-codereview-invocation.md](shared-pal-codereview-invocation.md). Already loaded, unchanged instructions need not be read twice. The common method applies even without profiles or specification documents.\n22\t\n23\t## Step 2: Resolve Scope from Filenames/Metadata\n24\t\n25\tUse status, diff statistics and filenames only to resolve scope. Honor an explicit range/file selection; otherwise choose unstaged changes, falling back to staged when none are unstaged. Keep that selector throughout preparation; do not silently combine scopes. If neither has changes, report that and request a range. A feature-directory listing may locate specifications; reading them belongs to investigation. Metadata scope does not open the investigation gate: the next actions are detection-rule reads, profile indexes and their checklists, never the diff or feature documents.\n26\t\n27\t## Step 3: Detect Profiles and Route All Checklists\n28\t\n29\tRun the shared detection procedure on scoped filenames. Read each entry in its **Known profiles** list and record the returned rule paths; do not discover names by listing directories or infer unread rules from profile names. An obvious extension match does not end enumeration. Resolve the plugin root from the loaded skill location (parent of `skills/`); construct absolute instruction paths rather than forwarding unresolved root tokens. For every active profile, read `profiles/<profile>/review-code/index.md` and select always-load, matching conditional and conservatively selected entries. Evaluate metadata predicates first; use only the bounded routing exception for content predicates. Do not defer conditionals until investigation.\n30\t\n31\tBatch the Known-profile reads together when supported, then reconcile expected versus returned rule paths before resolving profiles. An unread rule keeps routing incomplete; do not substitute a remembered extension rule for its returned contents.\n32\t\n33\tThe size bound alone does not authorize a read. Before any routing content read, name the unresolved predicate from an already returned detection rule/index and the candidate path it applies to. Extension/filename-only decisions require no source bytes. **Never run a full `git diff` for routing, even for a tiny diff:** inspect only a bounded candidate-file view needed by that declared predicate. With no unresolved content predicate, proceed directly to checklist loading.\n34\t\n35\tCollect `(profile, checklist, triggered_by)` records with absolute checklist paths and conservative-load notes.\n36\t\n37\t## Step 4: Read Every Checklist and Confirm Loading\n38\t\n39\tRead every selected file: an index or requested path is not returned instruction content. Do not batch these reads with investigation. Emit a compact loading checkpoint naming completed common-instruction reads and loaded checklists (or no active profiles). A failed or omitted instruction read keeps the gate closed; surface unreadable paths. Explicitly reconcile every resolved path with returned contents before proceeding. Loading the common files alone does not satisfy this gate. Do not read the diff, source or specifications in the same call as detection, index or checklist reads.\n40\t\n41\t## Step 5: Investigate and Prepare Evidence\n42\t\n43\tThis is the single entry point for subject-matter investigation. Locate and read available component README/contract documentation and relevant design/task contracts first; establish explicit intent and preserved invariants, labeling inference when no contract exists. Then read the selected full diff and changed files at the reviewed candidate, accounting for deletions. For staged review use index blobs; for a commit range use the selected candidate revision. Materialize those candidate sources outside the worktree when its current files differ; never pass unrelated worktree bytes as reviewed source. Large diffs (>500 lines) may be batched by module; report any context limit.\n44\t\n45\tWrite the selected diff to a temporary file outside the reviewed worktree, using `mktemp` and the same selector. Keep evidence until both reviewers and any follow-ups finish.\n46\t\n47\t### Context and affected paths\n48\t\n49\t- Build the Task Scope block from those contracts with the shared scope protocol, including the invoking implementation task even if still in progress. Without a related feature, use its no-task-scope variant.\n50\t- Establish or refresh the shared change context: intent/authority, task boundary, preserved behavior, delivery constraints, review base/candidate, separate compatibility baselines, scenarios and attributed verification results. Without a spec, infer provisional purpose from the request, diff, callers and tests; label it. Functional reasoning remains required.\n51\t- Trace affected behavior using the common method, including unchanged consumers and required compatibility combinations. Select source by the contract question it answers, not directory proximity. Search `kk:review-findings` and, for active programming-language profiles, `kk:lang-idioms`.\n52\t- Include concrete rejected-approach facts if useful, with their source; omit implementation-session narrative. Author explanations and test claims remain attributed evidence that reviewers may challenge.\n53\t- If exploration adds a target with another profile or conditional, pause its analysis and return to routing/loading before proceeding.\n54\t\n55\t### Historical source and evidence manifest\n56\t\n57\tWhen a comparison depends on source absent from candidate files or diff hunks, obtain the actual local Git blob or bounded excerpt. Materialize it in a read-only evidence bundle outside the reviewed worktree. For each item record repository identity, full revision, original path, blob/content hash, original line span and omitted/redacted extent. Distinguish source-level tag comparisons from verified deployment state. A missing source stays explicitly Unknown; a parent summary cannot replace it.\n58\t\n59\tBefore declaring a named baseline unavailable, inspect relevant repository documentation for its location and attempt to resolve the stated local ref/tag and source path in Git. If only a symbol/component is named, use a bounded history/tree lookup to locate it. Record the attempted ref/path and result. A separate-repository description or absence from the checkout does not prove absence from local history. Review-base mocks establish test assumptions, not the named provider's implementation; do not substitute them for available historical source.\n60\t\n61\tPrepare one categorized manifest for both reviewers, with absolute paths:\n62\t\n63\t1. **Instructions:** shared change context, common functional method, scope/knowledge protocols and all resolved profile checklists. These are criteria, not review targets.\n64\t2. **Review evidence:** selected diff, changed candidate source (mark deletions), unchanged entry points, dependencies, consumers, state contracts and relevant delivery configuration. Ten surrounding files is an initial selection budget only. Follow identified contract questions through focused additions or name the uncovered boundary; do not scan the whole repository.\n65\t3. **Historical evidence:** actual source files plus provenance manifest, distinct from candidate source.\n66\t4. **Intent/context:** applicable specification files, Task Scope and factual change-context record. The record may be inlined into both prompts or placed in a readable file provided to both; a filename alone is insufficient evidence of receipt.\n67\t\n68\tUse these same material facts and source artifacts for both reviewers. Resolve the PAL model via `listmodels`, preferring the most capable available model with reasoning support. Follow the shared PAL protocol's **code-review branch** for its file manifest and continuation.\n69\t\n70\tBefore dispatch, compare the manifest's instruction paths with the loading ledger. Every resolved checklist (including always-load templates), common method, shared context, scope and knowledge protocol must appear in both reviewers' instruction sets. Do not drop an always-load file as irrelevant or rebuild a smaller list from memory. Reconcile missing paths now; carry this checked manifest unchanged into the agent payload and PAL's expert continuation, adding focused evidence only when needed.\n71\t\n72\tThe required criteria set has **four common files plus every resolved profile file**: `functional-review.md`, `shared-change-context.md`, `shared-review-scope-protocol.md`, `shared-capy-knowledge-protocol.md`, and the resolved checklist paths. Verify both the count and exact resolved paths, using a set comparison through available tools when possible. The knowledge protocol remains included even when PAL cannot use Capy; that protocol specifies the unavailable-tool fallback. Keep criteria and source paths in the prepared manifest so submission does not depend on reconstructing either list from memory.\n73\t\n74\t## Step 6: Spawn Reviewers and Resolve Evidence Requests\n75\t\n76\tLaunch the independent `code-reviewer` and PAL step 1 in parallel when supported. Neither receives implementation conversation history or the other reviewer's conclusions.\n77\t\n78\t### Reviewer A \u2014 code-reviewer\n79\t\n80\tUse the Agent tool with `subagent_type: kk:code-reviewer`, description `Isolated code review`, and this payload. Expand plugin-relative checklist paths to absolute paths before dispatch; the reviewer requires resolved paths rather than environment variables.\n81\t\n82\t```\n83\tReview the selected changes using your full workflow. Load instructions before evidence.\n84\t\n85\t## Plugin Root\n86\t{resolved absolute plugin root}\n87\t\n88\t## Instructions\n89\t{absolute paths to shared change context, functional method, scope/knowledge protocols}\n90\t\n91\t## Active Profiles and Resolved Checklists\n92\t{flat (profile, absolute checklist path, triggered_by) records; conservative selections; or empty list}\n93\t\n94\t## Change Context and Spec Context\n95\t{factual record with provenance, assumptions, attributed execution results and spec paths;\n96\tor explicitly inferred intent when no spec exists}\n97\t\n98\t{Task Scope block}\n99\t\n100\t## Evidence Manifest\n101\t{selected diff path, candidate/source paths, historical-source paths and provenance manifest;\n102\treview selector and candidate identity; missing evidence}\n103\t\n104\t## Rejected Approaches\n105\t{concrete attributed facts only, or none}\n106\t\n107\tIndependently inspect the evidence and challenge inferences. Request specific missing source\n108\tby repository, revision, path/symbol and comparison needed. Report provisional findings and\n109\tlimits in your agent output format; do not claim test execution from source-only access.\n110\t```\n111\t\n112\t### Reviewer B \u2014 PAL codereview\n113\t\n114\tUse the shared protocol's two-step external review with the resolved model. Keep `step` lean: functional/correctness, security, architecture and design-intent framing; the same Task Scope and change-context facts; spec summary (or inferred purpose); categorized absolute file manifest. Identify the common method and profiles as criteria, historical source as baseline evidence and candidate source as the review target. The initial planning call's `relevant_files` contains only the selected diff; the expert continuation must attach the complete source/context/criteria bundle. Pass actual files through `relevant_files`, not pasted contents in `step`.\n115\t\n116\tAfter the initial response, complete the continuation; do not mistake the planning response for external findings. Preserve the native output and actual source-coverage evidence. Supplying paths does not prove the external reviewer read them.\n117\t\n118\tImmediately before the expert call, verify its `relevant_files` contains every path in the checked manifest. The diff-only planning payload is not the expert payload. A nonempty set of missing instruction/evidence paths blocks submission until those paths are added.\n119\t\n120\tBefore annotation, record each reviewer's outcome and observed source receipt. Inspect PAL's returned file-context/embedding metadata: zero or unspecified coverage without other observable receipt evidence stays unverified. Its final findings, the parent's `files_checked` list and correct-looking citations cannot establish receipt. Carry that limitation into the report before deciding whether any finding is corroborated.\n121\t\n122\t### Additional evidence and continuation\n123\t\n124\tFor either reviewer's evidence request, retain its origin and requested repository/revision/path or symbol and comparison. Obtain available local source using the historical-bundle rules. Resume the requesting code-reviewer with the original scope/context, its provisional findings and new source; if resume is unavailable, re-invoke it with that same package. Send equivalent relevant additions to PAL through its continuation, and to the other reviewer when its assessment depends on them. Include new guidance first if an addition activates a profile/conditional.\n125\t\n126\tBefore final reporting, reconcile every request with either supplied source or an actual failed/bounded lookup. Do not merely defer a locally answerable request because an earlier handoff assumed the source was unavailable. A request essential to acceptance that remains unanswered keeps its conclusion Unknown under the common verdict mapping.\n127\t\n128\tDo not pass one reviewer's findings to the other as evidence. Track what was supplied and which questions remain unanswered. Stop collecting once the identified comparison is settled or bounded investigation reaches an unavailable boundary. Unavailable source remains Unknown with a specific next action; do not expand reviewer permissions or seek production/network access automatically.\n129\t\n130\t### Failure and coverage limits\n131\t\n132\t- If PAL is unavailable or a call fails, retain useful available findings and proceed with the independent reviewer; disclose the failure.\n133\t- If the code-reviewer fails, retain PAL's usable findings and suggest standard review as a supplement.\n134\t- If both fail, stop isolated review and suggest standard review. Do not imply a completed review.\n135\t- Attempt best-effort parsing of malformed output; wholly unusable output is a failure.\n136\t- A successful response with zero or unverified source coverage cannot establish corroboration or broad approval. An adequately sourced clean result is not a failure merely because it found no defect.\n137\t\n138\t## Step 7: Annotate Findings\n139\t\n140\tThe parent supplies context, not replacement reviewer judgments. Do not assign dispositions such as Confirmed/Disputed or silently discard findings based on author preference.\n141\t\n142\t- Merge the same logical issue across lenses/reviewers once. Mark it **corroborated** only when both independently support it with relevant evidence. Otherwise preserve separate attribution and the coverage gap. Show differing severities side by side; PAL keeps its native format and severity.\n143\t- Add clearly labeled **Author context** where the implementation rationale helps: \u201cI chose X because Y,\u201d not \u201cThis finding is invalid.\u201d\n144\t- Label new parent observations **author-sourced**, distinct from independent findings.\n145\t- Use PAL continuation to clarify ambiguous external findings if needed. Preserve disagreements and provisional findings while awaiting source.\n146\t\n147\t## Step 8: Index Findings\n148\t\n149\tIndex systemic P0/P1 patterns as `kk:review-findings` on first encounter, from any attributed source, under the shared knowledge protocol. Do not index one-off mistakes. When none qualify, explicitly state \u201cNo findings to index.\u201d\n150\t\n151\t## Step 9: Present Report and Confirm Next Steps\n152\t\n153\tApply the common method's verdict mapping; keep code correctness, increment compatibility and release/activation readiness distinct. A hard acceptance/delivery violation cannot receive approval. Material Unknown evidence receives COMMENT absent a demonstrated blocker; an unrelated operational unknown need not downgrade a supported, explicitly scoped code verdict.\n154\t\n155\t```markdown\n156\t## Review Summary (Isolated Mode)\n157\t\n158\t**Reviewers**: code-reviewer ([actual model]), PAL ([actual model]); failures/coverage limits\n159\t**Files reviewed**: actual inspected coverage, distinct from files supplied\n160\t**Overall assessment**: APPROVE / REQUEST_CHANGES / COMMENT, with scope\n161\t**Intent and scope**: requirement source, task/request, diff selector and candidate state\n162\t**Baselines**: review base/candidate; separate named compatibility baseline\n163\t\n164\t### Behavior and Compatibility\n165\tInspected paths/scenarios, Supported / Blocked / Unknown / Not applicable conclusions,\n166\tsource references and limits. Attribute author results and inherited issues.\n167\t\n168\t### Corroborated Findings\n169\t- **[file:line]** Title \u27e8corroborated\u27e9\n170\t  - Profile: {profile} \u00b7 Checklist: {checklist} \u00b7 Triggered by: {signal}\n171\t  - code-reviewer: [P0\u2013P3, confidence/reasoning, trigger/path, expected/actual, consequence, correction]\n172\t  - PAL: [native finding]\n173\t  - Author context: [optional]\n174\t\n175\t### Code Reviewer Findings\n176\t{same evidence fields, code-reviewer attribution}\n177\t\n178\t### External Review Findings\n179\t{native output with attribution, coverage limits and optional author context}\n180\t\n181\t### Author-Sourced Findings\n182\t{explicit author-sourced label; generic profile labels when appropriate}\n183\t\n184\t### Outstanding Evidence and Prerequisites\n185\t{missing evidence, release/migration/activation conditions, owner, next action and durable\n186\ttracking location when deferred; separate them from demonstrated code defects}\n187\t```\n188\t\n189\tOmit empty finding sections, but always report behavior/compatibility coverage and limits, even with defects. Preserve useful findings during external failure or undercoverage without claiming corroboration or release safety from that external response.\n190\t\n191\tWhen neither actionable findings nor outstanding evidence/prerequisites required\n192\tby the reviewed task remain,\n193\tfinish with the scoped verdict and coverage; state that no changes are recommended.\n194\tDo not append a remediation menu or offer hypothetical future work. Otherwise,\n195\toffer only supported actions. For review-only work, obtain authorization before\n196\tediting; when the invoking implementation request already authorizes fixes,\n197\tfollow that scope without asking again. Unverified hard requirements still follow\n198\tthe common method's conditional verdict rules.\n199\t\n200\t## Step 10: Verify Outputs\n201\t\n202\t- [ ] Findings, actual scope/baselines, behavior/compatibility and evidence limits presented\n203\t- [ ] Both reviewer outcomes and coverage disclosed; additional evidence supplied or gaps named\n204\t- [ ] Systemic findings indexed, or \u201cNo findings to index\u201d noted\n205\t- [ ] Required next steps agreed/authorized, or clean review closed without new work\n206\t\n207\tRetain evidence needed for unresolved requests or a durable deferred review record. Once follow-ups and any required retention are complete, remove only temporary files/bundles created for this review.\n208\t"

The bounded routing exception below selects instructions only; it permits no behavioral analysis, findings, full-diff investigation, edits or tests.\n4\t\n5\tCopy and complete this checklist:\n6\t\n7\t```\n8\tCode Review Progress:\n9\t- [ ] Step 1: Load basic instructions\n10\t- [ ] Step 2: Select the diff from filenames/metadata\n11\t- [ ] Step 3: Detect profiles and route all checklists\n12\t- [ ] Step 4: Read every resolved checklist\n13\t- [ ] Step 5: Investigate behavior and compatibility\n14\t- [ ] Step 6: Apply profile and general checklists\n15\t- [ ] Step 7: Self-check and confidence assessment\n16\t- [ ] Step 8: Index findings\n17\t- [ ] Step 9: Present findings, coverage and verdict\n18\t- [ ] Step 10: Close the review or handle required next steps\n19\t- [ ] Step 11: Verify outputs\n20\t```\n21\t\n22\t### 1) Load basic instructions\n23\t\n24\tRead this entire process and [shared-capy-knowledge-protocol.md](shared-capy-knowledge-protocol.md), [shared-profile-detection.md](shared-profile-detection.md), [shared-review-scope-protocol.md](shared-review-scope-protocol.md), [shared-change-context.md](shared-change-context.md), and [functional-review.md](functional-review.md) before routing. [Packet preparation](packet-preparation.md) supplies these original contents and every Known-profile detection rule together; Read all parts completely, or use its direct-read fallback. Already loaded, unchanged instructions need not be read twice. The common context and functional method apply even without active profiles or specification documents.\n25\t\n26\t### 2) Select the diff from filenames/metadata\n27\t\n28\tUse `git status -sb` and diff statistics/filenames only. Honor an explicit user-selected scope/range; otherwise select unstaged changes, falling back to staged changes when none are unstaged. If neither exists, report no changes and request a range. Record the chosen selector and keep it for investigation. Do not silently combine staged and unstaged scopes.\n29\t\n30\tA large diff (>500 lines) needs focused batching, not an early content dump.\n31\tThis phase selects the Git diff only. Locating feature documents and determining\n32\ttask scope belong to investigation after checklist loading. Finding a task file\n33\tin a status/listing result does not make its requirements part of preparation.\n34\t\n35\t### 3) Detect profiles and route all checklists\n36\t\n37\tInvoke [shared-profile-detection.md](shared-profile-detection.md) with the scoped filenames. Read each known profile's `DETECTION.md` (directly or as complete original contents in the bootstrap packet) and evaluate its authoritative signals; do not stop enumerating after an obvious extension match. Path matches alone do not activate a profile. Multiple profiles may apply. Resolve the plugin root from this skill's loaded location (parent of `skills/`) and construct absolute profile paths; do not forward unresolved root tokens into tools.\n38\t\n39\tBatch the Known-profile reads together when supported, then reconcile expected paths with completed lookups. Record each rule's match/non-match or the shared procedure's explicit unavailable-file outcome; apply that procedure's ENOENT and root-resolution fallbacks. A skipped lookup leaves routing incomplete even when a file extension suggests an obvious profile. This prevents an assumed negative from hiding an additional applicable profile.\n40\t\n41\tFor every active profile, read its `profiles/<profile>/review-code/index.md`. Collect **Always load** entries. Evaluate filename/metadata **Load if:** predicates first. For declared content predicates in detection or conditional loading, inspect at most approximately 16 KiB per candidate file, solely to resolve the predicate; log predicate/path. Apply the detection protocol's YAML document rules where relevant. If a conditional cannot be decided within the bound, conservatively select its instruction. Do not defer any conditional until after full source investigation.\n42\t\n43\tCollect `(profile, checklist, triggered_by)` records for all matching/conservatively selected entries, carrying detection provenance and noting conservative selections. Indexes are authoritative; never hardcode checklist names. No profile means an empty list, not omission of the common method.\n44\t\n45\t### 4) Read every resolved checklist\n46\t\n47\tRead each selected `profiles/<profile>/review-code/<checklist>` using the resolved absolute plugin root, or Read the complete selected-checklist packet from [packet preparation](packet-preparation.md). An index read is routing, not checklist loading: every selected link requires its returned original content, even for a tiny diff. Keep the source paths as a loading ledger and mark them loaded only when complete read results arrive. Do not batch investigation commands with these reads.\n48\t\n49\tBefore proceeding, emit a compact loading checkpoint naming the completed common-instruction reads, every known profile's detection-lookup outcome, and the loaded checklist paths (or explicitly no active profiles). Any selected path without returned content keeps the gate closed; surface unreadable instructions and stop, except for detection fallbacks explicitly defined by the shared procedure. This checkpoint records evidence already obtained, not a promise to load guidance later. It catches accidental index-to-diff shortcuts without creating a separate artifact or replacing the actual read events.\n50\t\n51\t### 5) Investigate behavior and compatibility\n52\t\n53\tThis is the single entry point for subject-matter investigation. With methodology loaded:\n54\t\n55\t- Locate and read available component README/contract documentation and relevant requirements/task documents first; build task scope with the shared protocol and establish explicit intent and preserved invariants. Label inference where no contract exists. Distinguish review base/candidate from any release baseline.\n56\t- Then read the full diff using the selector from scope and re-read every changed file at the reviewed revision, accounting for deletions. Do not rely on an earlier conversation's contents. Use index blobs for staged review and the selected candidate revision for a commit range; do not silently substitute unrelated worktree contents.\n57\t- Apply the common functional method: trace changed behavior through relevant dependencies and unchanged consumers, exercise concrete scenarios, and assess relevant delivery combinations. Obtain actual historical source when needed; label provenance and unavailable evidence using the shared context rules.\n58\t- Search `kk:review-findings` for relevant patterns and, for active programming-language profiles, `kk:lang-idioms`. With no language results, optionally index a canonical idioms source under that label. Skip language lookup for non-language profiles.\n59\t\n60\tIf investigation adds targets covered by new profiles or newly applicable conditionals, pause analysis of those targets and return to routing/loading before continuing. Subsequent focused re-reads, reproductions and verification substantiate findings within this investigation; they do not create another full-diff preflight. Keep claims within actual access and evidence.\n61\t\n62\t### 6) Apply profile and general checklists\n63\t\n64\tApply every loaded `(profile, checklist, triggered_by)` record to the affected behavior and diff. Domain checklists supplement the common method. Tag findings with their profile/checklist origin and detection signal; use `Profile: generic \u00b7 Checklist: \u2014` and `Triggered by: \u2014` for common reasoning. Keep severity-major output and merge duplicate issues across lenses.\n65\t\n66\tGeneral guidance that applies regardless of profile \u2014 apply these categories on every diff, whether or not a profile-specific checklist covered them:\n67\t\n68\t- **SOLID / architecture:** SRP violations (overloaded modules with unrelated responsibilities), OCP (frequent edits to add behavior instead of extension points), LSP (subclasses that break expectations or require type checks), ISP (wide interfaces with unused methods), DIP (high-level logic tied to low-level implementations). When you propose a refactor, explain _why_ it improves cohesion/coupling and outline a minimal, safe split. If refactor is non-trivial, propose an incremental plan instead of a large rewrite.\n69\t- **Security / reliability:** XSS, injection (SQL/NoSQL/command), SSRF, path traversal; AuthZ/AuthN gaps, missing tenancy checks; secret leakage or API keys in logs/env/files; rate limits, unbounded loops, CPU/memory hotspots; unsafe deserialization, weak crypto, insecure defaults; race conditions, check-then-act, TOCTOU, missing locks. Call out both **exploitability** and **impact**.\n70\t- **Code quality:** error handling (swallowed exceptions, overly broad catch, missing handling, async errors); performance (N+1 queries, CPU-intensive ops in hot paths, missing cache, unbounded memory); boundary conditions (null/undefined, empty collections, numeric boundaries, off-by-one). Flag issues that may cause silent failures or production incidents.\n71\t- **Removal candidates:** propose deletion only when a demonstrated current cost or defect justifies it and required behavior is preserved. A pending task's unreachable scaffold or disabled feature is not, by itself, a removal candidate. Distinguish **safe delete now** vs **defer with plan** when removal is warranted. For warranted removals, provide concrete steps and proportionate validation checkpoints.\n72\t\n73\t### 7) Self-check and confidence assessment\n74\t\n75\tFor **each finding** from Steps 5\u20136:\n76\t\n77\t1. Re-read the relevant code and surrounding context independently.\n78\t2. Ask: **\"Could I be misreading the code?\"** \u2014 trace execution paths, check for runtime behavior, configuration, or framework conventions that might make this correct.\n79\t3. Ask: **\"Is this a real issue or a style preference?\"** \u2014 distinguish between bugs/risks and subjective choices that don't affect correctness or security.\n80\t4. Ask: **\"What's the actual impact?\"** \u2014 verify that the severity matches the real-world consequence, not just the theoretical violation.\n81\t5. Assign final confidence score (1\u2013100%) with **explicit reasoning** documenting:\n82\t   - What was verified\n83\t   - What evidence supports the finding\n84\t   - What uncertainty remains\n85\t6. Downgrade or **remove** findings that don't survive the self-check.\n86\t\n87\t### 8) Index findings\n88\t\n89\tIndex any P0/P1 findings that suggest a systemic or structural pattern (not isolated typos or one-off mistakes) as `kk:review-findings`. Index on first encounter \u2014 recurrence detection happens on the search side in future reviews.\n90\t\n91\t- If no P0/P1 systemic findings exist, explicitly note \"No findings to index\" and move on.\n92\t- This step is mandatory \u2014 do not skip it even if the review found no issues.\n93\t\n94\t### 9) Present results\n95\t\n96\t#### Output format\n97\t\n98\tStructure your review as follows:\n99\t\n100\t```markdown\n101\t## Code Review Summary\n102\t\n103\t**Files reviewed**: X files, Y lines changed\n104\t**Overall assessment**: [APPROVE / REQUEST_CHANGES / COMMENT]\n105\t**Intent and scope**: requirement source, current task/request, selected diff and candidate state\n106\t**Baselines**: review base/candidate; separate release baseline if applicable\n107\t\n108\t## Behavior and Compatibility\n109\t\n110\tInspected paths/scenarios, observed results and evidence limits. Include applicable\n111\tSupported / Blocked / Unknown / Not applicable conclusions with named baselines.\n112\tAttribute author-supplied results and inherited issues. Record outstanding evidence\n113\tor release prerequisites, next actions and durable tracking locations when deferred.\n114\t\n115\t---\n116\t\n117\t## Findings\n118\t\n119\t### P0 - Critical\n120\t\n121\t(none or list)\n122\t\n123\t### P1 - High\n124\t\n125\t- **[file:line]** Brief title\n126\t  - Profile: {profile_name} \u00b7 Checklist: {checklist_filename}\n127\t  - Triggered by: {signal_type} \u2014 {signal_description}\n128\t  - Trigger/path, expected vs actual result and consequence\n129\t  - Confidence: 90% - reasoning behind the confidence level\n130\t  - Suggested fix\n131\t\n132\t- **[another_file:line]** Brief title\n133\t  - Profile: generic \u00b7 Checklist: \u2014\n134\t  - Triggered by: \u2014\n135\t  - Trigger/path, expected vs actual result and consequence\n136\t  - Confidence: 60% - reasoning behind the confidence level\n137\t  - Suggested fix\n138\t\n139\t### P2 - Medium\n140\t\n141\t...\n142\t\n143\t### P3 - Low\n144\t\n145\t...\n146\t\n147\t---\n148\t\n149\t## Removal/Iteration Plan\n150\t\n151\t(if applicable)\n152\t\n153\tOnly include recommendations tied to a demonstrated issue or an identified,\n154\tcurrently required verification gap. A clean scoped review needs no extra work.\n155\t```\n156\t\n157\t**Inline comments**: Use this format for file-specific findings:\n158\t\n159\t```\n160\t::code-comment{file=\"path/to/file\" line=\"42\" severity=\"P1\"}\n161\tDescription of the issue and suggested fix.\n162\t::\n163\t```\n164\t\n165\tApply [functional-review.md](functional-review.md)'s verdict mapping: a hard acceptance/delivery violation cannot be approved; material Unknown evidence receives COMMENT absent a demonstrated blocker. Out-of-scope operational unknowns do not automatically downgrade an explicitly scoped code verdict. Preserve P0\u2013P3 impact severity and distinguish code correctness from release readiness.\n166\t\n167\t**Every review** reports coverage and limits, even with findings. When no issues are found, explicitly state:\n168\t\n169\t- What was checked\n170\t- Any areas not covered (e.g., \"Did not verify database migrations\")\n171\t- Specific residual risks or required verification gaps, when supported\n172\t\n173\t### 10) Close the review or handle required next steps\n174\t\n175\tWhen there are no actionable findings and no outstanding evidence/prerequisite\n176\trequired by the reviewed task, end with the scoped verdict and coverage. State\n177\tthat no changes are recommended. Omit a Next Steps menu, remediation choices and\n178\tinvitations to start pending work. A clean review is a complete result.\n179\t\n180\tWhen action is needed, list only substantiated findings and specific required\n181\tevidence or prerequisites. Ask which actions to take only when the current request\n182\thas not already authorized them; an invoking implementation request may already\n183\tcover fixes. Review-only work still does not authorize edits.\n184\t\n185\tApply the same evidence threshold to every suggestion, including optional notes\n186\tand proposed menu choices. A rejected hypothetical concern does not become valid\n187\tby moving it out of Findings or offering it as future preparation. Retain material\n188\tunknowns and the common method's conditional verdicts; this closing branch does\n189\tnot waive unverified requirements.\n190\t\n191\t### 11) Verify outputs\n192\t\n193\tBefore declaring the review complete, check each item in the **Required Outputs** section of SKILL.md:\n194\t\n195\t- [ ] Review report presented to user\n196\t- [ ] Intent, scope/baselines, inspected behavior, applicable compatibility conclusions and evidence limits reported\n197\t- [ ] P0/P1 systemic findings indexed as `kk:review-findings` (or explicitly noted \"No findings to index\")\n198\t- [ ] Required next steps handled, or clean review closed without proposing new work\n199\t\n200\tIf any item is unchecked, go back and complete it before proceeding.\n201\t"
  }

    "content": "1\t# Functional and operational review\n2\t\n3\tApply this common method after loading all instructions and resolved profile guidance. It complements SOLID, security and domain checklists on every change, including libraries, CLIs, infrastructure and agent instructions. Operational assessment applies where deployment, activation, persistence, startup, configuration or inter-component contracts are affected.\n4\t\n5\t## Trace the affected behavior\n6\t\n7\t1. Establish or reconstruct the record in [shared-change-context.md](shared-change-context.md). Locate and read the affected component's README or contract documentation before tracing behavior, when available. Record explicit outcomes, preserved invariants and delivery constraints with source locations; do not replace documented requirements with test expectations or invite a product decision already settled there. Label inferred intent when no contract exists. Use [shared-review-scope-protocol.md](shared-review-scope-protocol.md) to distinguish current work from genuinely pending functionality.\n8\t2. Describe relevant behavior before and after the change. Trace changed entry points/callers through meaningful dependencies to responses, side effects or durable state. Include unchanged consumers whose expectations may now fail. Read adjacent source to answer an identified contract/impact question; stop expanding once its contract and consequence are established, or name the uncovered boundary. A failed compound command does not establish source unavailability: retry permitted individual reads, while honoring explicit access denials; an isolated reviewer can request those reads through its parent. A file-count cap must not silently cut off a necessary trace.\n9\t3. Exercise that model with concrete success and boundary scenarios. Where applicable, include partial failure, retries, edits between attempts, cleanup and later reuse. Trace the documented scenario's actual state transitions through the unchanged consumer; a similar hypothetical sequence does not establish the documented failure/recovery path. For required transitions, cover the initial, relevant intermediate and final states against available fixtures; finding a failure in one state does not verify the others. Check which tests actually exercise the claimed contract and failure path. Green implementation-shaped tests or successful transport/status responses do not prove a successful business operation. Distinguish inspected source, executed tests and unverified assumptions; do not claim execution from source-only access.\n10\t4. Compare the approach with requirements and existing conventions. Challenge complexity by naming its cost and a simpler alternative preserving required behavior. Trace the proposed correction against documented invariants, including completed-operation/idempotent paths; retain necessary retry/recovery guarantees. Recommend work only for a demonstrated issue or specific uncovered requirement; do not duplicate existing test coverage. More abstraction is not inherently better; a hypothetical race is not automatically a new requirement.\n11\t5. Assess relevant compatibility combinations below, then apply profile expertise and substantiate findings. One issue spanning several lenses remains one finding.\n12\t\n13\tKeep a compact scenario record as you trace: **contract/source \u2192 initial state \u2192 operation or transition \u2192 expected result \u2192 candidate result \u2192 evidence/limit**. Populate it from the actual requirements before deciding which paths are covered; a finding does not complete unrelated required rows. Carry the relevant rows into the report, including supported controls and intermediate states. Small changes may use one sentence. For instruction changes, consumers may be another skill, an agent payload, a generator or a parser.\n14\t\n15\t## Compatibility and delivery\n16\t\n17\tIdentify combinations required by the actual delivery policy: for example, new consumer/old provider, old consumer/new provider, partly migrated data, disabled configuration, mixed versions or rollback. Select relevant combinations rather than an indiscriminate Cartesian product. Apply the shared context's historical-evidence rules when the decisive source is absent locally from the candidate.\n18\t\n19\tDeployment, activation and migration are distinct operations. A flag supports safety only after checking all affected startup, read, write and consumer paths obey it. Pending tasks cannot excuse a broken current flow. Conversely, unreachable unfinished paths need not be implemented for an otherwise compatible increment to ship. A documented prerequisite does not satisfy an explicit independently-releasable-main requirement.\n20\t\n21\tAssess the reviewed increment first. Expand to a production-to-candidate comparison only when a requested release assessment or relevant inherited change requires it and the baseline is available. Attribute inherited defects separately; distinguish introduced or worsened failures from pre-existing ones and supported reachability from hypothetical incidents. Bound any release conclusion when the full candidate cannot be assessed.\n22\t\n23\tFor each applicable dimension, state:\n24\t\n25\t- **Supported:** named scenarios and a named baseline support compatibility within stated evidence limits.\n26\t- **Blocked:** demonstrated incompatibility or a known unmet delivery requirement prevents the stated release.\n27\t- **Unknown:** material baseline, environment, consumer or data evidence is missing; name it and the next action.\n28\t- **Not applicable:** no consequence for that dimension, with a reason.\n29\t\n30\tSupported is not a production guarantee. Missing evidence is not an invented code defect. Preserve existing P0\u2013P3 severity according to actual impact; do not create a separate severity taxonomy.\n31\t\n32\tReserve P0 for demonstrated critical consequences needing immediate mitigation. A scoped regression or recoverable wrong value does not establish catastrophic or irreversible loss. Hard acceptance violations still require changes when their impact merits P1 or P2.\n33\t\n34\t## Verdict mapping\n35\t\n36\t| Evidence | Code-review verdict | Release assessment |\n37\t| --- | --- | --- |\n38\t| In-scope incompatibility caused or worsened by the change | One impact-rated finding. REQUEST_CHANGES for P0/P1 or a violated hard acceptance/delivery requirement; otherwise COMMENT for an actionable nonblocking issue. | Blocked for the affected required combination. |\n39\t| Known external rollout prerequisite, no demonstrated code defect | Assess code on its merits; APPROVE may be scoped explicitly to code. | Blocked until the prerequisite is met; document the dependency. |\n40\t| Unknown evidence essential to task acceptance or an explicitly requested release conclusion | COMMENT absent a defect warranting REQUEST_CHANGES. Never unconditionally approve the unverified requirement. | Unknown; name missing evidence and next action. |\n41\t| Unknown operational detail outside the requested/required assessment | Does not automatically downgrade a supported, explicitly scoped code verdict. | Unknown for that unassessed environment. |\n42\t| Supported or Not applicable | Apply existing severity/finding rules; neither overrides another defect. | Retain named baseline and limits. |\n43\t\n44\tCode approval with conditional release readiness cannot bypass a hard independent-delivery requirement. An unmet hard requirement stays open unless the user explicitly changes it or accepts an exception, recorded with rationale and prerequisites.\n45\t\n46\t## Evidence-qualified reporting\n47\t\n48\tEvery report, including one with findings, identifies intent, actual scope/base/candidate, inspected paths and important evidence limits. Include a compact behavior/compatibility assessment with applicable conclusions. For each behavioral finding provide trigger, affected path, expected/actual result, consequence and correction; preserve confidence reasoning, severity and profile/checklist labels. Separate inherited findings and author-attributed inputs.\n49\t\n50\tBefore presenting, reconcile every claimed inspection or execution with successful returned tool results. Denied reads, unread source and truncated results belong under missing coverage. A supported finding does not establish that the full diff was inspected or tests ran; remove unsupported coverage claims, including copied template wording.\n51\t\n52\tRecord outstanding evidence or release/migration/activation prerequisites and next actions; name their durable tracking location when deferred. Preserve disagreement and uncertainty. Reviewer agreement never replaces source evidence. In isolated reporting, retain native external findings and annotations, disclose failures or zero/incomplete source coverage, and do not claim corroboration or broad safety from an underfed external result. Useful available findings remain reportable.\n53\t"

    "type": "tool_result",
    "content": "1\t# Change context\n2\t\n3\tEstablish this context before implementation edits or review findings. It is a shared reasoning and handoff contract, not a mandatory new document. Scale it to the change: a few sentences for a helper fix, a compact scenario table for a cross-component mutation. Omit empty boilerplate; explain briefly why an operational dimension is irrelevant.\n4\t\n5\t## Minimum useful record\n6\t\n7\t| Field | Content |\n8\t| --- | --- |\n9\t| Intent and authority | Intended outcomes and constraints; source locations; explicit, inferred or unresolved status. |\n10\t| Change boundary | Current task/request, resolved diff scope, candidate revision/worktree state, affected components and deliberately pending functionality. |\n11\t| Existing behavior | Entry points, unchanged callers/consumers, state/data contracts and invariants to preserve. |\n12\t| Delivery constraints | Applicable repository policy, independent/ordered deployment expectations, flags, migrations, prerequisites and accepted exceptions. |\n13\t| Baselines | Review base and candidate; separately, relevant released/deployed revisions, configurations and data shapes; explicit unknowns. |\n14\t| Scenarios and evidence | Success, boundaries, failure/retry and lifecycle cases that apply; source/test bindings, observed results and unverified conditions. |\n15\t\n16\tUser decisions and applicable repository instructions establish requirements. Consult current design/task contracts and relevant linked issues without assuming an issue is newer or more authoritative. Explain consequential conflicts. Existing code establishes behavior, not product intent; tests can repeat an implementation's mistaken assumption. Without a specification, infer a provisional purpose from the request and inspected evidence and label it. Ask only when materially different interpretations prevent a useful judgment; continue independent investigation.\n17\t\n18\tNever equate the review base, local main, a stable tag and a deployed revision. Name the baseline actually inspected. Source compatibility with a tag does not verify a deployed environment. Missing documents or production access narrow conclusions; they neither disable functional reasoning nor create automatic access or approval requirements.\n19\t\n20\t## Producer and reviewer responsibilities\n21\t\n22\tThe producer supplies facts with file/symbol or revision references, separating assumptions, accepted decisions and unavailable evidence. Author explanations and claimed test results remain attributed inputs, not independent verification. Reviewers challenge inferences and request specific missing evidence.\n23\t\n24\tRefresh affected fields after scope/requirement changes or review fixes. On resume, inspect current repository state rather than trusting a stale handoff. In plan mode, record dated observations, sources, proposals, accepted decisions and unresolved actions in the current task's labeled **Execution context** subsection in `tasks.md`. Observations do not amend specifications; requirement changes need explicit user authorization with source and rationale. Standalone context stays conversational unless material work is deferred: use existing tracking or a concise repository-local review note naming owner (or unassigned), reason, next action and verification condition.\n25\t\n26\t## Historical evidence\n27\t\n28\tWhen compatibility depends on source absent from candidate files or diff hunks, the parent workflow obtains the relevant local Git blob or bounded excerpt. For independent reviewers, materialize actual source in a read-only evidence bundle outside the reviewed worktree. Label repository identity, full revision, original path, blob/content hash, original line span and omitted/redacted extent. Distinguish historical source from candidate files and methodology; give both reviewers the same relevant evidence, not merely the author's summary.\n29\t\n30\tA reviewer requests further evidence by naming repository, revision, path/symbol and the comparison needed. The parent supplies available local source through resume/reinvoke with original context and provisional findings, and supplies equivalent relevant additions through PAL continuation. Retain request provenance. This does not require reviewer shell permissions or authorize otherwise unapproved network/production access. Unavailable source or an inconclusive bounded investigation leaves the specific conclusion **Unknown**, with the missing evidence and next action.\n31\t"
  }

EVENT e180.0
{
  "id": "e180.0",
  "source_sequence": 180,
  "session_id": "bcb47d65-8609-4412-b2b2-ea3dba438aa6",

    "tool_use_id": "toolu_01BNqfjmrUcBRXTKUQMeDVcM",
    "type": "tool_result",
    "content": "1\t## Profile detection procedure\n2\t\n3\tSingle source of truth for computing the set of profiles active in the current context.\n4\tConsumed by six skills: `/kk:review-code`, `/kk:review-spec`, `/kk:design`, `/kk:implement`, `/kk:test`, and `/kk:document`.\n5\t\n6\tEvery profile under `klaude-plugin/profiles/<name>/` declares its own trigger rule in `DETECTION.md` using the mandatory three-section schema (`## Path signals`, `## Filename signals`, `## Content signals`).\n7\tThe shared procedure below applies the same algorithm against every profile's declared values.\n8\t\n9\t### Inputs per consuming skill\n10\t\n11\tNot every consumer has a diff available. Use the input listed for your skill:\n12\t\n13\t- **`/kk:review-code`** \u2014 git diff (staged, or an explicit commit range). Scope is\n14\t  the set of files the diff touches.\n15\t- **`/kk:review-spec`** \u2014 git diff when invoked standalone; the feature directory's\n16\t  full file list when invoked by `/kk:implement` (spec review runs over the whole\n17\t  feature, not just the current task's diff).\n18\t- **`/kk:test`** \u2014 git diff mid-feature, OR the feature directory's file list\n19\t  post-implementation.\n20\t- **`/kk:implement`** \u2014 the current sub-task's target file list, augmented by the\n21\t  diff accumulated so far in the feature.\n22\t- **`/kk:design`** \u2014 **no file list available** (implementation does not yet exist).\n23\t  Detection uses a user-declared or keyword-inferred signal instead; see\n24\t  [The `/kk:design` interaction pattern](#the-design-interaction-pattern) below.\n25\t- **`/kk:document`** \u2014 feature directory's current file list; diff optional.\n26\t\n27\t### The `/kk:design` interaction pattern\n28\t\n29\tThe design phase runs before any code exists, so file-based detection is impossible. Detection uses idea-prose keyword matching against tokens declared in each profile's `DETECTION.md`.\n30\t\n31\t**Algorithm:**\n32\t\n33\t1. **Collect tokens.** Iterate \u00a7Known profiles. For each `<name>`, `Read` `${TOOLBOX_PLUGIN_ROOT}/profiles/<name>/DETECTION.md`. If the file has no `## Design signals` section, skip \u2014 that profile does not participate in design-phase detection. Otherwise, parse `display_name` and `tokens` from the section.\n34\t2. **Build union.** Collect all declared tokens into a single set, each tagged by its source profile name and `display_name`.\n35\t3. **Match.** Check the idea prose against the union. Matching is case-insensitive, whole-word (so `pod` in \"podcast\" does not fire).\n36\t4. **Confirm.** On match, surface a confirmation prompt per matched profile:\n37\t   *\"This appears to be a {display_name} feature. Activate the {profile_name} profile?\"* \u2014 let the user confirm yes/no. When multiple profiles match, confirm each independently.\n38\t5. **Fallback.** If no token matches but the idea is **ambiguous** \u2014 names infrastructure, deployment, runtime, or platform concerns without naming a specific technology (e.g., _\"add a caching layer for the service\"_, _\"build a CI pipeline\"_, _\"deploy to production\"_); or includes overloaded tokens that collide across domains \u2014 build the fallback prompt dynamically from all profiles that declare `## Design signals`:\n39\t   *\"Does this feature involve {display_name_1, display_name_2, ...}? If yes, which?\"*\n40\t\n41\tConfirmation is required \u2014 the /kk:design skill never auto-activates a profile silently. The narrow per-profile token sets avoid noisy false positives from tokens that overload across domains.\n42\t\n43\tOnce activated, subsequent design-phase steps treat the profile as active in the same record shape produced by file-based detection (see \u00a7Output shape).\n44\t\n45\t### Known profiles\n46\t\n47\tThis is the authoritative enumeration of profile `<name>`s \u2014 do NOT try discover profiles via any other means.\n48\tAn explicit list is boring, deterministic, and unambiguous; runtime filesystem enumeration against the plugin tree has proven unreliable.\n49\t\n50\t- `go`\n51\t- `python`\n52\t- `java`\n53\t- `js_ts`\n54\t- `kotlin`\n55\t- `k8s`\n56\t- `k8s-operator`\n57\t- `skill-md`\n58\t\n59\t### Algorithm\n60\t\n61\tThis procedure reads files under the plugin root. The main agent resolves the plugin root from its shell variable `$TOOLBOX_PLUGIN_ROOT`; a Read-only sub-agent uses the absolute plugin-root path injected into its prompt under `## Plugin Root` (see its agent definition). Substitute that resolved path for the plugin-root prefix in every `\u2026/profiles/\u2026` read below.\n62\t\n63\t1. **Iterate profiles.** For each \u00a7Known profiles `<name>`:\n64\t   1. Use the `Read` tool on `${TOOLBOX_PLUGIN_ROOT}/profiles/<name>/DETECTION.md`.\n65\t   2. If `Read` fails with ENOENT (profile name in list but directory missing \u2014 a stale list entry), skip silently and move on.\n66\t   3. If `Read` succeeds, parse the declared `## Path signals`, `## Filename signals`, and `## Content signals` sections.\n67\t\n68\t2. **Evaluate in cost order.** For each input file, check signals in this order: path \u2192 filename \u2192 content. Cheapest first.\n69\t3. **Apply the authority rule.** A file activates the profile only if a **filename signal** OR **content signal** matches.\n70\t   A path-only match does NOT activate. Paths are a pre-filter that promotes files to \"candidates\"; authoritative activation requires filename or content confirmation.\n71\t   A file that matches NO path signal is still evaluated against filename and content signals \u2014 path pre-filtering is a cost hint, not a gate.\n72\t   (Otherwise a `Chart.yaml` at a non-standard path would be missed.)\n73\t4. **Bound content inspection.** Read at most ~16 KB per file when evaluating content signals.\n74\t   Multi-document YAML is inspected per `---`-separated block \u2014 a file may have five blocks, and only the third need match for the file to activate the profile.\n75\t5. **Collect records.** Accumulate one record per matched profile with the triggering files and the signal descriptions that fired.\n76\t\n77\t### Tool choice\n78\t\n79\tA consumer may consolidate instruction reads into bounded packets containing\n80\tthe complete, unmodified source bodies, original paths and hashes. Read every\n81\tpacket part completely before applying those instructions; the original paths\n82\tstill determine link resolution and detection provenance. A manifest, checksum,\n83\trequested read or truncated packet alone does not establish loading. This\n84\tchanges packaging only: evaluate every Known-profile rule and each index\n85\tconditional exactly as below. Direct file reads remain the default fallback.\n86\t\n87\t- Single file at `${TOOLBOX_PLUGIN_ROOT}/\u2026` \u2192 `Read`. This is what the algorithm uses.\n88\t- Enumeration across profiles \u2192 iterate the \u00a7Known profiles list, `Read` each. Never `Glob` (cwd-scoped, misses outside-cwd paths).\n89\t\n90\t### Two dimensions: cost vs authority\n91\t\n92\tSignals live on two axes that point in different directions. Keep them separate in your mental model:\n93\t\n94\t- **Evaluation cost** (cheapest first): path < filename < content.\n95\t  Path globs touch only the path string;\n96\t  filename matches are exact string compares;\n97\t  content inspection opens the file.\n98\t- **Authority** (most authoritative first): filename \u2248 content > path.\n99\t  A filename or content match activates the profile; a path-only match does not.\n100\t  Filename and content are equally authoritative, but filename resolves first at runtime \u2014 a filename match short-circuits content inspection for that file.\n101\t\n102\tEvaluating cheapest-first optimizes work. Applying authority correctly prevents false positives from incidental path matches \u2014 a stray `manifests/` directory in a Go project does not make the project Kubernetes.\n103\t\n104\t### Plugin-root resolution failure\n105\t\n106\tIf every `Read` attempt in Algorithm step 1 fails \u2014 i.e., the plugin root could not be resolved (the variable is unset for the main agent, or no `## Plugin Root` path was provided to a sub-agent) or the paths do not exist \u2014 the procedure cannot continue.\n107\t\n108\tOn that failure:\n109\t\n110\t1. Emit an actionable error pointing to `CLAUDE.md` \u00a7Profile Conventions.\n111\t2. Return an empty result set so the calling skill falls back to generic guidance rather than panicking.\n112\t3. Do not retry; do not silently guess a path.\n113\t\n114\tConsumers inherit this check by invoking the shared procedure \u2014 no skill re-implements it.\n115\t\n116\t### Output shape\n117\t\n118\tA list of records, one per matched profile:\n119\t\n120\t```\n121\t[\n122\t  {\n123\t    profile: \"<name>\",                     // directory name under profiles/\n124\t    triggered_by: [\n125\t      \"filename: Chart.yaml\",              // signal type + matched value\n126\t      \"content: apiVersion+kind in block 2\"\n127\t    ],\n128\t    files: [\n129\t      \"path/to/file1.yaml\",\n130\t      \"path/to/file2.yaml\"\n131\t    ]\n132\t  },\n133\t  ...\n134\t]\n135\t```\n136\t\n137\tField semantics:\n138\t\n139\t- `profile` \u2014 the directory name under `profiles/` (e.g., `go`, `python`, `k8s`).\n140\t  Used downstream to resolve `profiles/<profile>/<phase>/index.md`,\n141\t  where `<phase>` is the profile phase subdirectory named identically to the calling skill:\n142\t  `review-code/`, `review-spec/`, `design/`, `implement/`, `test/`, or `document/`.\n143\t- `triggered_by` \u2014 which signal type fired and the specific value that matched.\n144\t  For debugging and for explaining detection to the user; never used as the key for profile lookup.\n145\t- `files` \u2014 the subset of input files that activated this profile.\n146\t  Skills use this to scope behavior (e.g., `helm lint` runs only on files triggered under Helm filename signals, not on every YAML in the diff).\n147\t\n148\tWhen no profile matches, return the empty list `[]`. The caller falls back to generic guidance, identical to today's \"no language detected\" path.\n149\t"
  }

    "type": "tool_result",
    "content": "1\t## Review scope protocol\n2\t\n3\tReviewers need task status alongside the diff and design docs. Without scope, they flag code-that-isn't-written-yet as `MISSING_IMPL` (spec review) or as missing functionality (code review). Standard review uses this scope locally; isolated review injects the same artifact into sub-agent and external-reviewer prompts. The existing /kk:review-spec payload remains sufficient; no additional change-context record is required by this protocol.\n4\t\n5\t**Code-review timing:** apply this protocol's task-document reads during the\n6\tpost-instruction investigation phase, after every resolved checklist is loaded.\n7\tEarly diff selection uses filenames and Git metadata; it is distinct from\n8\tdetermining task scope. Reading this protocol does not authorize early inspection\n9\tof a discovered task file's requirements. The calling workflow still governs\n10\tany bounded inspection needed solely for a declared routing predicate.\n11\t\n12\t### Input shapes\n13\t\n14\tThe review workflow must handle three invocation shapes:\n15\t\n16\t1. **Invoked from `/kk:implement`** \u2014 `/docs/feat/wip/[feature]/` exists with `design.md`, `implementation.md`, `tasks.md`. The current task id is known (the one just finished). Use full scope filtering.\n17\t2. **Invoked directly inside a feature** \u2014 `/docs/feat/wip/[feature]/` exists but no current task is explicitly supplied. Derive scope from `tasks.md` status fields (`done` = in scope, `pending`/`in-progress` = out of scope).\n18\t3. **Invoked on an arbitrary diff** \u2014 no `/docs/feat/wip/` directory relates to the diff. Skip the scope artifact entirely and note \"No task scope available\" in the reviewer prompt.\n19\t\n20\t### Scope artifact format\n21\t\n22\tWhen shape 1 or 2 applies, build this block and inject it into every reviewer prompt (sub-agent prompt template and the external-reviewer `step` framing):\n23\t\n24\t```\n25\t## Task Scope\n26\t\n27\tReview mode: {mid-implementation | post-implementation}\n28\t\n29\tIn scope (review these):\n30\t- Task {id}: {title} \u2014 status: done\n31\t- [...more done tasks if applicable]\n32\t\n33\tOut of scope (pending \u2014 DO NOT flag as missing):\n34\t- Task {id}: {title} \u2014 status: {pending|in-progress}\n35\t- [...more pending tasks]\n36\t\n37\tThe feature's design docs describe the full end state. Pending tasks are expected gaps in the current diff \u2014 treat their absence from the code as intentional, not as a finding. You may still flag issues within the in-scope tasks even if they reference pending tasks (e.g., a broken interface contract). Pending work does not excuse current regressions or incompatibility: assess whether the reviewed increment preserves existing flows and meets applicable delivery requirements. Genuinely unreachable unfinished paths need not be implemented merely because they are planned.\n38\t```\n39\t\n40\tWhen shape 3 applies, use:\n41\t\n42\t```\n43\t## Task Scope\n44\t\n45\tNo task scope available \u2014 review the diff on its own merits without assuming any feature-level design doc.\n46\t```\n47\t\n48\t### Determining mode\n49\t\n50\t- **mid-implementation** \u2014 at least one task in `tasks.md` has status `pending` or `in-progress`.\n51\t- **post-implementation** \u2014 all tasks are `done`.\n52\t\n53\tIn shape 1, the invoking workflow (`/kk:implement`) may pass the current task id directly; that task is automatically in scope even if `tasks.md` hasn't been updated yet.\n54\t\n55\t### Interpretation guidance for reviewers\n56\t\n57\tThe reviewer prompt must also carry this instruction (agent definitions should state it; the workflow relays it implicitly via the scope block):\n58\t\n59\t- Do **not** report missing-implementation findings for items covered only by out-of-scope tasks.\n60\t- Do still report real issues *within* the in-scope tasks, including ones that affect future pending work (e.g., a public API the pending task will depend on is shaped wrongly).\n61\t- A current consumer that already requires a pending provider change remains reviewable, including when the larger feature is disabled. Distinguish deployment from activation and check the actual affected paths; a flag or a future task alone proves neither reachability nor safety.\n62\t- Attribute introduced/worsened behavior separately from inherited defects. Do not invent a current regression or require pending work when the increment remains compatible under its actual delivery contract.\n63\t- If a finding would be valid *only* after pending work lands, mention it under \"Areas Not Covered\" instead of the P0\u2013P3 sections.\n64\t"
  }
