    "content": "1\t## pal codereview invocation\n2\t\n3\t`pal codereview` is a **multi-turn** tool. The initial call prepares review; complete the external continuation before treating it as an expert result. Select the caller branch below before constructing the payload. Common transport, native-output and failure rules apply to both branches.\n4\t\n5\t### Code-review branch\n6\t\n7\tFor /kk:review-code, pass the same material scope, change-context facts, criteria and source evidence as the independent code-reviewer. Functional reasoning still applies without specifications; label inferred purpose and unresolved evidence. Do not import implementation-session history or the other reviewer's conclusions.\n8\t\n9\t#### Assembling `relevant_files`\n10\t\n11\tAll paths are absolute and readable. Categorize them by role:\n12\t\n13\t1. **Diff:** temporary file containing the selected diff.\n14\t2. **Candidate source:** changed files at the selected worktree/index/revision, accounting for deletions. When current files differ, use materialized candidate snapshots with original-path/revision provenance.\n15\t3. **Affected behavior:** relevant entry points, dependencies, unchanged consumers, state/data contracts and delivery configuration. Ten surrounding files is an initial selection budget, not a correctness boundary. Follow identified contract questions through focused additions/continuations or report the uncovered boundary; do not scan whole repositories.\n16\t4. **Historical source:** actual local Git blobs or bounded excerpts required for compatibility, plus their provenance manifest (repository, full revision, original path, blob/content hash, line span, omitted/redacted extent). Prepare read-only bundles outside the reviewed worktree. A parent summary cannot replace source absent from candidate files/diff hunks.\n17\t5. **Review criteria:** the common functional method, shared change-context and scope/knowledge protocols, plus resolved profile checklists. Resolve paths from the caller's known installation root; identify these as instructions to apply, not source to review.\n18\t6. **Intent/context:** relevant design/implementation/task documents and the factual change-context record if file-based. Inline the same Task Scope block and factual context into `step` when not supplied as files; keep baselines and attributed test results explicit.\n19\t\n20\tFor code review, submit only the selected **diff file** in the initial planning call's `relevant_files`. Supply the complete categorized bundle, including historical source and criteria, in the external continuation. This keeps the planning request small and makes newly embedded source coverage observable at the expert step; files carried through conversation history can otherwise be deduplicated from its new-file count. Do not omit the continuation's source bundle or treat a zero count as proof of either receipt or absence without other evidence.\n21\t\n22\tRetain the same relevant source additions for both reviewers. For a missing-evidence request, the parent obtains available local source, then uses continuation with the new files and provenance. Name the comparison still needed; unavailable evidence stays Unknown with a next action. Do not expand access permissions automatically.\n23\t\n24\t### Document-review branch\n25\t\n26\tFor /kk:review-design, document inputs remain sufficient. No Git diff, code-review change context, historical bundle or code profile is required.\n27\t\n28\t- **Documents:** every in-scope design, implementation or task document; these are the review targets.\n29\t- **Related source:** existing files the design references, where available, for technical context.\n30\t\n31\tFrame the review around technical soundness, completeness, consistency, edge cases, failure modes, constraints and ambiguity. Preserve the caller's document scope; do not treat planned code as a missing current implementation. Use its own review criteria. The common call sequence below remains identical.\n32\t\n33\t### Step 1 \u2014 initial call\n34\t\n35\t| Parameter | Value |\n36\t|---|---|\n37\t| `model` | Most capable available model from `pal listmodels` |\n38\t| `step` | Framing, applicable scope/context summary and categorized file manifest below |\n39\t| `step_number` | `1` |\n40\t| `total_steps` | `2` |\n41\t| `next_step_required` | `true` |\n42\t| `review_validation_type` | `\"external\"` |\n43\t| `thinking_mode` | `\"max\"` |\n44\t| `review_type` | `\"full\"` |\n45\t| `findings` | `\"Initial submission for review. No findings yet.\"` |\n46\t| `relevant_files` | Code review: selected diff file only. Document review: in-scope documents and related source as supplied by the caller |\n47\t| `confidence` | `\"exploring\"` |\n48\t\n49\t#### `step` content\n50\t\n51\tKeep framing lean; file contents belong in `relevant_files`, not pasted into `step`:\n52\t\n53\t1. Review purpose and applicable criteria. For code, include intended behavior, preserved contracts and applicable compatibility as well as security/architecture/quality.\n54\t2. Scope and factual context: code's Task Scope/change context/spec summary, or document review's selected documents and purpose.\n55\t3. Categorized absolute-path manifest so the model distinguishes criteria, candidate targets, historical evidence and intent. For code review, identify the initial diff and the full bundle to be attached at continuation; do not label later files as already submitted. The expert continuation's manifest, for example:\n56\t\n57\t   ```\n58\t   Files provided via relevant_files:\n59\t   - Diff: /tmp/kk-review-code-a1b2c3.patch\n60\t   - Candidate source: /project/src/client.py\n61\t   - Affected behavior: /project/src/settings.py\n62\t   - Historical source: /tmp/kk-evidence-a1b2c3/provider.py\n63\t   - Historical provenance: /tmp/kk-evidence-a1b2c3/manifest.json\n64\t   - Review criteria: /plugin/skills/review-code/functional-review.md, ...\n65\t   - Intent/context: /project/docs/feat/wip/settings/design.md, ...\n66\t   ```\n67\t\n68\tDocument callers use **Documents** and **Related source** instead. Paths sent are not proof of source receipt: retain actual embedding/read evidence returned by the tool and distinguish it from requested coverage.\n69\t\n70\t### Step 2 \u2014 continuation call\n71\t\n72\tUse the initial response's `continuation_id`. Reattach the relevant source/criteria paths so the expert stage has the required inputs; record only genuinely inspected files in `files_checked` if that field is used, distinguishing the caller's checks from external receipt.\n73\t\n74\t| Parameter | Value |\n75\t|---|---|\n76\t| `model` | Same model as step 1 |\n77\t| `continuation_id` | From step 1 response |\n78\t| `step` | Produce expert findings using the selected branch, supplied criteria and evidence; identify inspected coverage and unresolved comparisons |\n79\t| `step_number` | `2` |\n80\t| `total_steps` | `2` |\n81\t| `next_step_required` | `false` |\n82\t| `review_validation_type` | `\"external\"` |\n83\t| `relevant_files` | Required branch-specific files, including any new evidence/provenance |\n84\t| `findings` | Copy/summarize step 1's returned findings, with attribution; do not insert the independent reviewer's conclusions |\n85\t| `confidence` | Match actual evidence; do not assert high confidence merely because this is the final step |\n86\t\n87\tFor focused later evidence/clarification, continue the same review using its returned continuation identifier, the new files and the outstanding comparison. Preserve request provenance and provisional findings. If continuation is unavailable, disclose it and re-invoke with the original scope/context and the requesting reviewer's own provisional results plus additions; do not silently claim an uninterrupted review.\n88\t\n89\t### Parallel execution with sub-agents\n90\t\n91\tLaunch step 1 alongside the independent reviewer when supported, then complete PAL's continuation. Supply equivalent relevant evidence additions to both without sharing their conclusions.\n92\t\n93\t### Failure modes and coverage\n94\t\n95\t- No available model or tool/call failure: disclose it and retain the other reviewer's useful findings.\n96\t- Initial call succeeds but continuation fails: retain any useful returned findings as provisional and label the incomplete external review.\n97\t- Zero findings with demonstrated relevant source coverage may be a clean review; zero findings alone prove neither success nor failure.\n98\t- Zero embedded/read source, unverified receipt or materially incomplete coverage: disclose that limit. Do not claim independent corroboration or broad code/release approval from the underfed response. Retain useful source-backed findings from the available reviewer and qualify unresolved conclusions.\n99\t\n100\tPAL output remains in **native format**, with attribution. Do not remap its severity or finding types. Agreement does not substitute for source evidence. Keep temporary artifacts through evidence requests and required retention, then clean up only files created for this review.\n101\t"
  }

    "content": "1\t# Kubernetes \u2014 detection\n2\t\n3\tDeclares when the `k8s` profile activates on a given set of files. Consumed by `klaude-plugin/skills/_shared/profile-detection.md`. Detection is additive: multiple profiles may activate on the same diff (e.g., `go` + `k8s`).\n4\t\n5\tEvaluation follows the shared cost-ordered procedure (path \u2192 filename \u2192 content). Authority runs filename \u2248 content > path: filename or content signals activate the profile; path alone never does, it only promotes a file to a candidate.\n6\t\n7\t## Path signals\n8\t\n9\tCase-insensitive substring match anywhere in the file's path. Pre-filter only \u2014 a path hit alone does NOT activate the profile.\n10\t\n11\t- `k8s/`\n12\t- `manifests/`\n13\t- `charts/`\n14\t- `kustomize/`\n15\t- `deploy/`\n16\t- `templates/`\n17\t\n18\t## Filename signals\n19\t\n20\tAuthoritative: any match activates the profile. Filename matches short-circuit content inspection for the matched file.\n21\t\n22\t- `Chart.yaml` (exact) \u2192 Helm chart root.\n23\t- Any filename starting with `values` (e.g., `values.yaml`, `values.yml`, `values-prod.yaml`, `values-prod-v2-final.yaml`) when the containing directory also contains `Chart.yaml` \u2192 Helm values by adjacency. The `values*` glob has no upper bound on the wildcard; the adjacency rule (sibling `Chart.yaml` in the same directory) is the binding constraint. The match is filename-plus-adjacency only \u2014 file content is not inspected, so a file named `values-backup.yaml` next to a `Chart.yaml` activates regardless of what it actually contains.\n24\t- Any file with extension `.yaml`, `.yml`, or `.tpl` inside `<dir>/templates/` where `<dir>` itself contains a `Chart.yaml` as a direct child \u2192 Helm template. The binding constraint is that the `templates/` directory must sit *directly* next to a `Chart.yaml` \u2014 i.e., at a chart root or a subchart root under `<parent>/charts/<subchart>/`. A `templates/` nested elsewhere in the tree (e.g., `docs/templates/`, `ci/templates/`) does NOT activate this rule even when a `Chart.yaml` sits at the repository root, because `docs/` and `ci/` do not themselves contain a `Chart.yaml`. This avoids the monorepo false-positive where a repo-root umbrella `Chart.yaml` would otherwise claim every `templates/` directory in the tree. It still avoids the trap where a standalone edit to `<chart-root>/templates/deployment.yaml` contains `{{ if ... }}` directives before any `apiVersion:` and would otherwise fail the content signal.\n25\t- Exact filenames `kustomization.yaml`, `kustomization.yml`, or `Kustomization` \u2192 Kustomize.\n26\t\n27\t## Content signals\n28\t\n29\tAuthoritative for generic YAML files (`.yaml` or `.yml`) not already caught by a filename signal. Inspection is bounded to the first ~16 KB per file; large generated manifests beyond that bound are not inspected.\n30\t\n31\t- Split the file on `---` document separators. For each `---`-separated document block, check for a top-level `apiVersion:` AND a top-level `kind:` \u2014 parsed as YAML mapping keys at zero indent, not as substrings inside block scalars (`|`, `>`) or comments. A block satisfying both is a Kubernetes manifest document.\n32\t- One matching document activates the profile for that file. The first document need not match \u2014 a file whose second or later document is the only K8s document still activates.\n33\t- A `.yaml` / `.yml` file with no matching document in any block \u2192 not Kubernetes. (It may still match another profile; generic YAML belongs to no profile by default.)\n34\t\n35\t---\n36\t\n37\t## Multi-profile behavior\n38\t\n39\tThe Kubernetes profile is **additive**. It coexists with programming-language profiles or any other IaC profile on the same diff. When Go source files sit alongside Kubernetes manifests, both `go` and `k8s` activate; downstream skills consult both profiles' content and emit findings grouped by `(profile, checklist)`.\n40\t\n41\t## Design signals\n42\t\n43\tdisplay_name: Kubernetes\n44\ttokens:\n45\t  - Kubernetes\n46\t  - K8s\n47\t  - Helm chart\n48\t  - kubectl\n49\t  - kustomize\n50\t  - manifest.yaml\n51\t  - Deployment resource\n52\t  - StatefulSet\n53\t  - DaemonSet\n54\t  - CronJob\n55\t\n56\t## Dockerfile non-trigger\n57\t\n58\tA Dockerfile on its own \u2014 even under a `deploy/` or `k8s/` directory \u2014 does NOT activate the `k8s` profile. Dockerfiles match no filename signal here (they are not `Chart.yaml` / `values*.yaml` / `kustomization.yaml`) and no content signal (they do not contain `apiVersion:` + `kind:`). When a Dockerfile appears in the same diff as Kubernetes manifests, `k8s` activates on the manifests' signals; the Dockerfile itself is not reviewed by this profile. A future container profile may own Dockerfiles independently.\n59\t"
  }

Inspection is bounded to the first ~16 KB per file.\n26\t\n27\t- A `Makefile` containing the literal string `controller-gen` OR a target named `manifests` (line starting with `manifests:`) \u2192 kubebuilder/operator-sdk generated Makefile with CRD/RBAC generation targets.\n28\t- A Go source file (`.go` extension) containing an import of `sigs.k8s.io/controller-runtime` \u2192 controller-runtime dependency, the standard library for writing Kubernetes controllers.\n29\t\n30\t## Design signals\n31\t\n32\tdisplay_name: Kubernetes Operator\n33\ttokens:\n34\t  - operator\n35\t  - controller\n36\t  - kubebuilder\n37\t  - controller-runtime\n38\t  - CRD authoring\n39\t  - custom resource definition authoring\n40\t  - reconciliation loop\n41\t"

A `SKILL.md` at the repo root does NOT claim every file in the repository \u2014 files in subdirectories that have their own `SKILL.md` are scoped to that nearer ancestor. Files outside any `SKILL.md`-containing ancestor do not activate.\n22\t\n23\t**Edge cases and non-triggers:**\n24\t- Test-fixture `SKILL.md` files inside `evals/test-files/` are legitimate detection targets \u2014 they *are* skill files.     "content": "1\t# Security and Reliability Checklist\n2\t\n3\t## Contents\n4\t- Input/Output Safety (XSS, injection, SSRF, path traversal, unsafe deserialization)\n5\t- AuthN/AuthZ\n6\t- JWT & Token Security\n7\t- Secrets and PII\n8\t- Supply Chain & Dependencies\n9\t- CORS & Headers\n10\t- Runtime Risks (unbounded ops, GIL contention, resource exhaustion)\n11\t- Cryptography\n12\t- Race Conditions (shared state, TOCTOU, database concurrency, distributed)\n13\t- Data Integrity\n14\t\n15\t## Input/Output Safety\n16\t\n17\t- **XSS**: Unsafe HTML injection, Jinja2 `|safe` filter or `Markup()` bypassing auto-escaping, raw string interpolation in HTML responses\n18\t- **Injection**: SQL injection via f-strings or `%` formatting instead of parameterized queries; command injection via `os.system`, `subprocess.run(shell=True)` with user input\n19\t- **SSRF**: User-controlled URLs reaching internal services without allowlist validation\n20\t- **Path traversal**: User input in file paths without sanitization (`../` attacks), `os.path.join` with absolute user input bypassing base directory\n21\t- **Unsafe deserialization**: Using `pickle.loads`, `yaml.load` (without `SafeLoader`), or `eval`/`exec` on untrusted input\n22\t- **Code injection via imports**: User input reaching `__import__`, `importlib.import_module`, or dynamic module loading\n23\t\n24\t## AuthN/AuthZ\n25\t\n26\t- Missing tenant or ownership checks for read/write operations\n27\t- New endpoints without auth guards or RBAC enforcement\n28\t- Trusting client-provided roles/flags/IDs\n29\t- Broken access control (IDOR - Insecure Direct Object Reference)\n30\t- Session fixation or weak session management\n31\t\n32\t## JWT & Token Security\n33\t\n34\t- Algorithm confusion attacks (accepting `none` or `HS256` when expecting `RS256`)\n35\t- Weak or hardcoded secrets\n36\t- Missing expiration (`exp`) or not validating it\n37\t- Sensitive data in JWT payload (tokens are base64, not encrypted)\n38\t- Not validating `iss` (issuer) or `aud` (audience)\n39\t\n40\t## Secrets and PII\n41\t\n42\t- API keys, tokens, or credentials in code/config/logs\n43\t- Secrets in git history or environment variables exposed to client\n44\t- Excessive logging of PII or sensitive payloads\n45\t- Missing data masking in error messages\n46\t\n47\t## Supply Chain & Dependencies\n48\t\n49\t- Unpinned dependencies allowing malicious updates (missing version pins in `requirements.txt`/`pyproject.toml`)\n50\t- Dependency confusion (private package name collision on PyPI)\n51\t- Importing from untrusted sources without integrity checks\n52\t- Outdated dependencies with known CVEs\n53\t\n54\t## CORS & Headers\n55\t\n56\t- Overly permissive CORS (`Access-Control-Allow-Origin: *` with credentials)\n57\t- Missing security headers (CSP, X-Frame-Options, X-Content-Type-Options)\n58\t- Exposed internal headers or stack traces\n59\t\n60\t## Runtime Risks\n61\t\n62\t- Unbounded loops, recursive calls, or large in-memory buffers\n63\t- Missing timeouts, retries, or rate limiting on external calls\n64\t- Blocking operations on the event loop (sync I/O in `async` context)\n65\t- Resource exhaustion (file handles, connections, memory)\n66\t- **`assert` stripped in optimized mode**: `python -O` removes all `assert` statements \u2014 never use `assert` for security/input validation\n67\t- ReDoS (Regular Expression Denial of Service)\n68\t\n69\t## Cryptography\n70\t\n71\t- Weak algorithms (MD5, SHA1 for security purposes)\n72\t- Hardcoded IVs or salts\n73\t- Using encryption without authentication (ECB mode, no HMAC)\n74\t- Insufficient key length\n75\t- Using `==` for secret/token comparison instead of `hmac.compare_digest` (leaks timing information)\n76\t\n77\t## Race Conditions\n78\t\n79\tRace conditions are subtle bugs that cause intermittent failures and security vulnerabilities. Pay special attention to:\n80\t\n81\t### Shared State Access\n82\t- Multiple threads accessing shared variables without locks (GIL does not protect multi-step operations)\n83\t- Global state or module-level mutables modified concurrently\n84\t- Lazy initialization without proper locking\n85\t- Non-thread-safe collections used in concurrent context\n86\t\n87\t### Check-Then-Act (TOCTOU)\n88\t- `if (exists) then use` patterns without atomic operations\n89\t- `if (authorized) then perform` where authorization can change\n90\t- File existence check followed by file operation\n91\t- Balance check followed by deduction (financial operations)\n92\t- Inventory check followed by order placement\n93\t- `tempfile.mktemp()` is deprecated (TOCTOU race) \u2014 use `tempfile.mkstemp()` or `NamedTemporaryFile`\n94\t\n95\t### Database Concurrency\n96\t- Missing optimistic locking (`version` column, `updated_at` checks)\n97\t- Missing pessimistic locking (`SELECT FOR UPDATE`)\n98\t- Read-modify-write without transaction isolation\n99\t- Counter increments without atomic operations (`UPDATE SET count = count + 1`)\n100\t- Unique constraint violations in concurrent inserts\n101\t\n102\t### Distributed Systems\n103\t- Missing distributed locks for shared resources\n104\t- Leader election race conditions\n105\t- Cache invalidation races (stale reads after writes)\n106\t- Event ordering dependencies without proper sequencing\n107\t- Split-brain scenarios in cluster operations\n108\t\n109\t### Common Patterns to Flag\n110\t```python\n111\t# Dangerous patterns:\n112\t\n113\t# TOCTOU\n114\tif not os.path.exists(path):\n115\t    open(path, \"w\").write(data)\n116\t\n117\t# Read-modify-write\n118\tvalue = cache.get(key)\n119\tvalue += 1\n120\tcache.set(key, value)\n121\t\n122\t# Check-then-act\n123\tif user.balance >= amount:\n124\t    user.balance -= amount\n125\t    user.save()\n126\t```\n127\t\n128\t### Questions to Ask\n129\t- \"What happens if two requests hit this code simultaneously?\"\n130\t- \"Is this operation atomic or can it be interrupted?\"\n131\t- \"What shared state does this code access?\"\n132\t- \"How does this behave under high concurrency?\"\n133\t\n134\t## Data Integrity\n135\t\n136\t- Missing transactions, partial writes, or inconsistent state updates\n137\t- Weak validation before persistence (implicit type conversion issues)\n138\t- Missing idempotency for retryable operations\n139\t- Lost updates due to concurrent modifications\n140\t"
  }

EVENT e224.0

  "id": "e224.0",

    "content": "1\t# SOLID Smell Prompts\n2\t\n3\t## SRP (Single Responsibility)\n4\t\n5\t- File owns unrelated concerns (e.g., HTTP + DB + domain rules in one file)\n6\t- Large class/module with low cohesion or multiple reasons to change\n7\t- Functions that orchestrate many unrelated steps\n8\t- God objects that know too much about the system\n9\t- Circular imports between modules (signals tangled responsibilities \u2014 causes `ImportError`/`AttributeError` at runtime)\n10\t- **Ask**: \"What is the single reason this module would change?\"\n11\t\n12\t## OCP (Open/Closed)\n13\t\n14\t- Adding a new behavior requires editing many `if/elif` chains (or `match/case` blocks)\n15\t- Feature growth requires modifying core logic rather than extending\n16\t- No plugin/strategy/hook points for variation\n17\t- **Ask**: \"Can I add a new variant without touching existing code?\"\n18\t\n19\t## LSP (Liskov Substitution)\n20\t\n21\t- Subclass checks for concrete type via `isinstance` or raises for base method\n22\t- Overridden methods weaken preconditions or strengthen postconditions\n23\t- Subclass ignores or no-ops parent behavior\n24\t- **Ask**: \"Can I substitute any subclass without the caller knowing?\"\n25\t\n26\t## ISP (Interface Segregation)\n27\t\n28\t- ABCs/Protocols with many methods, most unused by implementers\n29\t- Callers depend on broad interfaces for narrow needs\n30\t- `NotImplementedError` stubs in subclasses\n31\t- **Ask**: \"Do all implementers use all methods?\"\n32\t\n33\t## DIP (Dependency Inversion)\n34\t\n35\t- High-level logic depends on concrete IO, storage, or network types\n36\t- Hard-coded implementations instead of abstractions or injection\n37\t- Import chains that couple business logic to infrastructure\n38\t- **Ask**: \"Can I swap the implementation without changing business logic?\"\n39\t\n40\t---\n41\t\n42\t## Common Code Smells (Beyond SOLID)\n43\t\n44\t| Smell | Signs |\n45\t|-------|-------|\n46\t| **Long method** | Function > 30 lines, multiple levels of nesting |\n47\t| **Feature envy** | Method uses more data from another class than its own |\n48\t| **Data clumps** | Same group of parameters passed together repeatedly |\n49\t| **Primitive obsession** | Using strings/numbers instead of domain types |\n50\t| **Shotgun surgery** | One change requires edits across many files |\n51\t| **Divergent change** | One file changes for many unrelated reasons |\n52\t| **Dead code** | Unreachable or never-called code |\n53\t| **Speculative generality** | Abstractions for hypothetical future needs |\n54\t| **Magic numbers/strings** | Hardcoded values without named constants |\n55\t| **Expensive `@property`** | Property getter performs I/O, DB queries, or heavy computation \u2014 callers expect attribute access to be cheap |\n56\t\n57\t---\n58\t\n59\t## Refactor Heuristics\n60\t\n61\t1. **Split by responsibility, not by size** - A small file can still violate SRP\n62\t2. **Introduce abstraction only when needed** - Wait for the second use case\n63\t3. **Keep refactors incremental** - Isolate behavior before moving\n64\t4. **Preserve behavior first** - Add tests before restructuring\n65\t5. **Name things by intent** - If naming is hard, the abstraction might be wrong\n66\t6. **Prefer composition over inheritance** - Multiple inheritance creates MRO complexity and tight coupling\n67\t7. **Make illegal states unrepresentable** - Use types to enforce invariants\n68\t"
  }
}

EVENT e226.0
{
  "id": "e226.0",
  "source_sequence": 226,
  "session_id": "bcb47d65-8609-4412-b2b2-ea3dba438aa6",
  "actor": "main",
  "parent_tool_use_id": null,
  "block": {
    "tool_use_id": "toolu_01VFYwwSR1kTfDkd3qkvWvvM",
    "type": "tool_result",
    "content": "1\t# Code Quality Checklist\n2\t\n3\t## Contents\n4\t- Error Handling (anti-patterns, best practices, questions)\n5\t- Performance & Caching (CPU, database/IO, caching, memory)\n6\t- Boundary Conditions (None handling, empty collections, numeric, string)\n7\t\n8\t## Error Handling\n9\t\n10\t### Anti-patterns to Flag\n11\t\n12\t- **Swallowed exceptions**: Empty `except` blocks or catch with only logging. Example:\n13\t  ```python\n14\t  try: ...\n15\t  except Exception: pass              # Silent failure\n16\t  try: ...\n17\t  except Exception as e: print(e)     # Log and forget, no re-raise\n18\t  ```\n19\t- **Overly broad except**: Catching bare `Exception` or `BaseException` instead of specific types\n20\t- **Error information leakage**: Stack traces or internal details exposed to users\n21\t- **Missing error handling**: No try-except around fallible operations (I/O, network, parsing)\n22\t- **Async error handling**: Unhandled exceptions in `asyncio` tasks, missing `try/except` in coroutines, fire-and-forget tasks without error callbacks\n23\t\n24\t### Best Practices to Check\n25\t\n26\t- [ ] Errors are caught at appropriate boundaries\n27\t- [ ] Error messages are user-friendly (no internal details exposed)\n28\t- [ ] Errors are logged with sufficient context for debugging\n29\t- [ ] Async task exceptions are retrieved or handled (not silently dropped)\n30\t- [ ] Fallback behavior is defined for recoverable errors\n31\t- [ ] Critical errors trigger alerts/monitoring\n32\t\n33\t### Questions to Ask\n34\t- \"What happens when this operation fails?\"\n35\t- \"Will the caller know something went wrong?\"\n36\t- \"Is there enough context to debug this error?\"\n37\t\n38\t---\n39\t\n40\t## Performance & Caching\n41\t\n42\t### CPU-Intensive Operations\n43\t\n44\t- **Expensive operations in hot paths**: `re.compile` in loops, JSON parsing in hot paths, crypto in loops\n45\t- **Blocking the event loop**: Sync I/O or heavy computation in `async` functions without `run_in_executor`\n46\t- **Unnecessary recomputation**: Same calculation done multiple times\n47\t- **Missing caching**: Pure functions called repeatedly with same inputs, no `@functools.lru_cache` or `@functools.cache`\n48\t- **GIL limitation for CPU-bound work**: `threading` does not parallelize CPU-bound tasks due to the GIL \u2014 use `multiprocessing` or `concurrent.futures.ProcessPoolExecutor`\n49\t\n50\t### Database & I/O\n51\t\n52\t- **N+1 queries**: Loop that makes a query per item instead of batch\n53\t  ```python\n54\t  # Bad: N+1\n55\t  for id in ids:\n56\t      user = db.execute(\"SELECT * FROM users WHERE id = %s\", (id,)).fetchone()\n57\t\n58\t  # Good: Batch\n59\t  users = db.execute(\"SELECT * FROM users WHERE id IN %s\", (tuple(ids),)).fetchall()\n60\t  ```\n61\t- **Missing indexes**: Queries on unindexed columns\n62\t- **Over-fetching**: SELECT * when only few columns needed\n63\t- **No pagination**: Loading entire dataset into memory\n64\t\n65\t### Caching Issues\n66\t\n67\t- **Missing cache for expensive operations**: Repeated API calls, DB queries, computations\n68\t- **Cache without TTL**: Stale data served indefinitely\n69\t- **Cache without invalidation strategy**: Data updated but cache not cleared\n70\t- **Cache key collisions**: Insufficient key uniqueness\n71\t- **Caching user-specific data globally**: Security/privacy issue\n72\t\n73\t### Memory\n74\t\n75\t- **Unbounded collections**: Lists/dicts that grow without limit\n76\t- **Large object retention**: Holding references preventing GC, circular references\n77\t- **String concatenation in loops**: Use `\"\".join(parts)` instead\n78\t- **Loading large files entirely**: Use streaming/iterator patterns instead\n79\t- **Mutable default arguments**: `def foo(items=[])` \u2014 default list is shared across all calls, mutating it causes cross-call contamination\n80\t- **Generator exhaustion**: Iterating a generator/iterator twice silently produces no results on the second pass\n81\t- **Missing context managers**: Not using `with` for files, DB connections, locks \u2014 resources leak on exceptions\n82\t- **Late binding closures**: Lambdas/closures in loops capture the variable by reference, not by value \u2014 all closures see the final loop value\n83\t- **`__del__` unreliability**: `__del__` is not guaranteed to run \u2014 never rely on it for cleanup; use context managers or `atexit`\n84\t\n85\t### Questions to Ask\n86\t- \"What's the time complexity of this operation?\"\n87\t- \"How does this behave with 10x/100x data?\"\n88\t- \"Is this result cacheable? Should it be?\"\n89\t- \"Can this be batched instead of one-by-one?\"\n90\t\n91\t---\n92\t\n93\t## Boundary Conditions\n94\t\n95\t### None Handling\n96\t\n97\t- **Missing None checks**: Accessing attributes on potentially `None` objects (causes `AttributeError`)\n98\t- **Truthiness confusion**: `if value:` when `0`, `\"\"`, `[]`, `{}` are valid values\n99\t- **Excessive None checks**: Deep chains of `if x is not None and x.y is not None` hiding structural issues\n100\t- **`is` vs `==` confusion**: Using `is` to compare values instead of `==` \u2014 works unreliably due to interning (e.g., `x is \"hello\"` may pass for small strings but fail for others)\n101\t- **None vs missing inconsistency**: Mixed usage of `None` and sentinel values without clear convention\n102\t\n103\t### Empty Collections\n104\t\n105\t- **Empty list not handled**: Code assumes list has items\n106\t- **Empty dict edge case**: Iteration or key access on empty dict\n107\t- **First/last element access**: `items[0]` or `items[-1]` without length check\n108\t\n109\t### Numeric Boundaries\n110\t\n111\t- **Division by zero**: Missing check before division (raises `ZeroDivisionError`)\n112\t- **Integer overflow**: Python handles big ints natively, but watch for C-extension or struct boundaries\n113\t- **Floating point comparison**: Using `==` instead of `math.isclose`\n114\t- **Negative values**: Index or count that shouldn't be negative\n115\t- **Off-by-one errors**: Loop bounds, slicing, pagination\n116\t\n117\t### String Boundaries\n118\t\n119\t- **Empty string**: Not handled as edge case\n120\t- **Whitespace-only string**: Passes truthiness check but is effectively empty\n121\t- **Very long strings**: No length limits causing memory/display issues\n122\t- **Unicode edge cases**: Emoji, RTL text, combining characters\n123\t\n124\t### Common Patterns to Flag\n125\t\n126\t```python\n127\t# Dangerous: no None check (AttributeError if user or profile is None)\n128\tname = user.profile.name\n129\t\n130\t# Dangerous: list access without check (IndexError if items is empty)\n131\tfirst = items[0]\n132\t\n133\t# Dangerous: division without check (ZeroDivisionError if count is 0)\n134\tavg = total / count\n135\t\n136\t# Dangerous: truthiness check excludes valid values\n137\tif value:  ...   # fails for 0, \"\", [], {}, False\n138\t```\n139\t\n140\t### Questions to Ask\n141\t- \"What if this is None?\"\n142\t- \"What if this collection is empty?\"\n143\t- \"What's the valid range for this number?\"\n144\t- \"What happens at the boundaries (0, -1, sys.maxsize)?\"\n145\t"
  }
}

    "content": "1\t# Functional and operational review\n2\t\n3\tApply this common method after loading all instructions and resolved profile guidance. It complements SOLID, security and domain checklists on every change, including libraries, CLIs, infrastructure and agent instructions. Operational assessment applies where deployment, activation, persistence, startup, configuration or inter-component contracts are affected.\n4\t\n5\t## Trace the affected behavior\n6\t\n7\t1. Establish or reconstruct the record in [shared-change-context.md](shared-change-context.md). Locate and read the affected component's README or contract documentation before tracing behavior, when available. Record explicit outcomes, preserved invariants and delivery constraints with source locations; do not replace documented requirements with test expectations or invite a product decision already settled there. Label inferred intent when no contract exists. Use [shared-review-scope-protocol.md](shared-review-scope-protocol.md) to distinguish current work from genuinely pending functionality.\n8\t2. Describe relevant behavior before and after the change. Trace changed entry points/callers through meaningful dependencies to responses, side effects or durable state. Include unchanged consumers whose expectations may now fail. Read adjacent source to answer an identified contract/impact question; stop expanding once its contract and consequence are established, or name the uncovered boundary. A failed compound command does not establish source unavailability: retry permitted individual reads, while honoring explicit access denials; an isolated reviewer can request those reads through its parent. A file-count cap must not silently cut off a necessary trace.\n9\t3. Exercise that model with concrete success and boundary scenarios. Where applicable, include partial failure, retries, edits between attempts, cleanup and later reuse. Trace the documented scenario's actual state transitions through the unchanged consumer; a similar hypothetical sequence does not establish the documented failure/recovery path. For required transitions, cover the initial, relevant intermediate and final states against available fixtures; finding a failure in one state does not verify the others. Check which tests actually exercise the claimed contract and failure path. Green implementation-shaped tests or successful transport/status responses do not prove a successful business operation. Distinguish inspected source, executed tests and unverified assumptions; do not claim execution from source-only access.\n10\t4. Compare the approach with requirements and existing conventions. Challenge complexity by naming its cost and a simpler alternative preserving required behavior. Trace the proposed correction against documented invariants, including completed-operation/idempotent paths; retain necessary retry/recovery guarantees. Recommend work only for a demonstrated issue or specific uncovered requirement; do not duplicate existing test coverage. More abstraction is not inherently better; a hypothetical race is not automatically a new requirement.\n11\t5. Assess relevant compatibility combinations below, then apply profile expertise and substantiate findings. One issue spanning several lenses remains one finding.\n12\t\n13\tKeep a compact scenario record as you trace: **contract/source \u2192 initial state \u2192 operation or transition \u2192 expected result \u2192 candidate result \u2192 evidence/limit**. Populate it from the actual requirements before deciding which paths are covered; a finding does not complete unrelated required rows. Carry the relevant rows into the report, including supported controls and intermediate states. Small changes may use one sentence. For instruction changes, consumers may be another skill, an agent payload, a generator or a parser.\n14\t\n15\t## Compatibility and delivery\n16\t\n17\tIdentify combinations required by the actual delivery policy: for example, new consumer/old provider, old consumer/new provider, partly migrated data, disabled configuration, mixed versions or rollback. Select relevant combinations rather than an indiscriminate Cartesian product. Apply the shared context's historical-evidence rules when the decisive source is absent locally from the candidate.\n18\t\n19\tDeployment, activation and migration are distinct operations. A flag supports safety only after checking all affected startup, read, write and consumer paths obey it. Pending tasks cannot excuse a broken current flow. Conversely, unreachable unfinished paths need not be implemented for an otherwise compatible increment to ship. A documented prerequisite does not satisfy an explicit independently-releasable-main requirement.\n20\t\n21\tAssess the reviewed increment first. Expand to a production-to-candidate comparison only when a requested release assessment or relevant inherited change requires it and the baseline is available. Attribute inherited defects separately; distinguish introduced or worsened failures from pre-existing ones and supported reachability from hypothetical incidents. Bound any release conclusion when the full candidate cannot be assessed.\n22\t\n23\tFor each applicable dimension, state:\n24\t\n25\t- **Supported:** named scenarios and a named baseline support compatibility within stated evidence limits.\n26\t- **Blocked:** demonstrated incompatibility or a known unmet delivery requirement prevents the stated release.\n27\t- **Unknown:** material baseline, environment, consumer or data evidence is missing; name it and the next action.\n28\t- **Not applicable:** no consequence for that dimension, with a reason.\n29\t\n30\tSupported is not a production guarantee. Missing evidence is not an invented code defect. Preserve existing P0\u2013P3 severity according to actual impact; do not create a separate severity taxonomy.\n31\t\n32\tReserve P0 for demonstrated critical consequences needing immediate mitigation. A scoped regression or recoverable wrong value does not establish catastrophic or irreversible loss. Hard acceptance violations still require changes when their impact merits P1 or P2.\n33\t\n34\t## Verdict mapping\n35\t\n36\t| Evidence | Code-review verdict | Release assessment |\n37\t| --- | --- | --- |\n38\t| In-scope incompatibility caused or worsened by the change | One impact-rated finding. REQUEST_CHANGES for P0/P1 or a violated hard acceptance/delivery requirement; otherwise COMMENT for an actionable nonblocking issue. | Blocked for the affected required combination. |\n39\t| Known external rollout prerequisite, no demonstrated code defect | Assess code on its merits; APPROVE may be scoped explicitly to code. | Blocked until the prerequisite is met; document the dependency. |\n40\t| Unknown evidence essential to task acceptance or an explicitly requested release conclusion | COMMENT absent a defect warranting REQUEST_CHANGES. Never unconditionally approve the unverified requirement. | Unknown; name missing evidence and next action. |\n41\t| Unknown operational detail outside the requested/required assessment | Does not automatically downgrade a supported, explicitly scoped code verdict. | Unknown for that unassessed environment. |\n42\t| Supported or Not applicable | Apply existing severity/finding rules; neither overrides another defect. | Retain named baseline and limits. |\n43\t\n44\tCode approval with conditional release readiness cannot bypass a hard independent-delivery requirement. An unmet hard requirement stays open unless the user explicitly changes it or accepts an exception, recorded with rationale and prerequisites.\n45\t\n46\t## Evidence-qualified reporting\n47\t\n48\tEvery report, including one with findings, identifies intent, actual scope/base/candidate, inspected paths and important evidence limits. Include a compact behavior/compatibility assessment with applicable conclusions. For each behavioral finding provide trigger, affected path, expected/actual result, consequence and correction; preserve confidence reasoning, severity and profile/checklist labels. Separate inherited findings and author-attributed inputs.\n49\t\n50\tBefore presenting, reconcile every claimed inspection or execution with successful returned tool results. Denied reads, unread source and truncated results belong under missing coverage. A supported finding does not establish that the full diff was inspected or tests ran; remove unsupported coverage claims, including copied template wording.\n51\t\n52\tRecord outstanding evidence or release/migration/activation prerequisites and next actions; name their durable tracking location when deferred. Preserve disagreement and uncertainty. Reviewer agreement never replaces source evidence. In isolated reporting, retain native external findings and annotations, disclose failures or zero/incomplete source coverage, and do not claim corroboration or broad safety from an underfed external result. Useful available findings remain reportable.\n53\t"

    "type": "tool_result",
    "content": "1\t## Review scope protocol\n2\t\n3\tReviewers need task status alongside the diff and design docs. Without scope, they flag code-that-isn't-written-yet as `MISSING_IMPL` (spec review) or as missing functionality (code review). Standard review uses this scope locally; isolated review injects the same artifact into sub-agent and external-reviewer prompts. The existing /kk:review-spec payload remains sufficient; no additional change-context record is required by this protocol.\n4\t\n5\t**Code-review timing:** apply this protocol's task-document reads during the\n6\tpost-instruction investigation phase, after every resolved checklist is loaded.\n7\tEarly diff selection uses filenames and Git metadata; it is distinct from\n8\tdetermining task scope. Reading this protocol does not authorize early inspection\n9\tof a discovered task file's requirements. The calling workflow still governs\n10\tany bounded inspection needed solely for a declared routing predicate.\n11\t\n12\t### Input shapes\n13\t\n14\tThe review workflow must handle three invocation shapes:\n15\t\n16\t1. **Invoked from `/kk:implement`** \u2014 `/docs/feat/wip/[feature]/` exists with `design.md`, `implementation.md`, `tasks.md`. The current task id is known (the one just finished). Use full scope filtering.\n17\t2. **Invoked directly inside a feature** \u2014 `/docs/feat/wip/[feature]/` exists but no current task is explicitly supplied. Derive scope from `tasks.md` status fields (`done` = in scope, `pending`/`in-progress` = out of scope).\n18\t3. **Invoked on an arbitrary diff** \u2014 no `/docs/feat/wip/` directory relates to the diff. Skip the scope artifact entirely and note \"No task scope available\" in the reviewer prompt.\n19\t\n20\t### Scope artifact format\n21\t\n22\tWhen shape 1 or 2 applies, build this block and inject it into every reviewer prompt (sub-agent prompt template and the external-reviewer `step` framing):\n23\t\n24\t```\n25\t## Task Scope\n26\t\n27\tReview mode: {mid-implementation | post-implementation}\n28\t\n29\tIn scope (review these):\n30\t- Task {id}: {title} \u2014 status: done\n31\t- [...more done tasks if applicable]\n32\t\n33\tOut of scope (pending \u2014 DO NOT flag as missing):\n34\t- Task {id}: {title} \u2014 status: {pending|in-progress}\n35\t- [...more pending tasks]\n36\t\n37\tThe feature's design docs describe the full end state. Pending tasks are expected gaps in the current diff \u2014 treat their absence from the code as intentional, not as a finding. You may still flag issues within the in-scope tasks even if they reference pending tasks (e.g., a broken interface contract). Pending work does not excuse current regressions or incompatibility: assess whether the reviewed increment preserves existing flows and meets applicable delivery requirements. Genuinely unreachable unfinished paths need not be implemented merely because they are planned.\n38\t```\n39\t\n40\tWhen shape 3 applies, use:\n41\t\n42\t```\n43\t## Task Scope\n44\t\n45\tNo task scope available \u2014 review the diff on its own merits without assuming any feature-level design doc.\n46\t```\n47\t\n48\t### Determining mode\n49\t\n50\t- **mid-implementation** \u2014 at least one task in `tasks.md` has status `pending` or `in-progress`.\n51\t- **post-implementation** \u2014 all tasks are `done`.\n52\t\n53\tIn shape 1, the invoking workflow (`/kk:implement`) may pass the current task id directly; that task is automatically in scope even if `tasks.md` hasn't been updated yet.\n54\t\n55\t### Interpretation guidance for reviewers\n56\t\n57\tThe reviewer prompt must also carry this instruction (agent definitions should state it; the workflow relays it implicitly via the scope block):\n58\t\n59\t- Do **not** report missing-implementation findings for items covered only by out-of-scope tasks.\n60\t- Do still report real issues *within* the in-scope tasks, including ones that affect future pending work (e.g., a public API the pending task will depend on is shaped wrongly).\n61\t- A current consumer that already requires a pending provider change remains reviewable, including when the larger feature is disabled. Distinguish deployment from activation and check the actual affected paths; a flag or a future task alone proves neither reachability nor safety.\n62\t- Attribute introduced/worsened behavior separately from inherited defects. Do not invent a current regression or require pending work when the increment remains compatible under its actual delivery contract.\n63\t- If a finding would be valid *only* after pending work lands, mention it under \"Areas Not Covered\" instead of the P0\u2013P3 sections.\n64\t"
  }

    "content": "1\t# Security and Reliability Checklist\n2\t\n3\t## Contents\n4\t- Input/Output Safety (XSS, injection, SSRF, path traversal, unsafe deserialization)\n5\t- AuthN/AuthZ\n6\t- JWT & Token Security\n7\t- Secrets and PII\n8\t- Supply Chain & Dependencies\n9\t- CORS & Headers\n10\t- Runtime Risks (unbounded ops, GIL contention, resource exhaustion)\n11\t- Cryptography\n12\t- Race Conditions (shared state, TOCTOU, database concurrency, distributed)\n13\t- Data Integrity\n14\t\n15\t## Input/Output Safety\n16\t\n17\t- **XSS**: Unsafe HTML injection, Jinja2 `|safe` filter or `Markup()` bypassing auto-escaping, raw string interpolation in HTML responses\n18\t- **Injection**: SQL injection via f-strings or `%` formatting instead of parameterized queries; command injection via `os.system`, `subprocess.run(shell=True)` with user input\n19\t- **SSRF**: User-controlled URLs reaching internal services without allowlist validation\n20\t- **Path traversal**: User input in file paths without sanitization (`../` attacks), `os.path.join` with absolute user input bypassing base directory\n21\t- **Unsafe deserialization**: Using `pickle.loads`, `yaml.load` (without `SafeLoader`), or `eval`/`exec` on untrusted input\n22\t- **Code injection via imports**: User input reaching `__import__`, `importlib.import_module`, or dynamic module loading\n23\t\n24\t## AuthN/AuthZ\n25\t\n26\t- Missing tenant or ownership checks for read/write operations\n27\t- New endpoints without auth guards or RBAC enforcement\n28\t- Trusting client-provided roles/flags/IDs\n29\t- Broken access control (IDOR - Insecure Direct Object Reference)\n30\t- Session fixation or weak session management\n31\t\n32\t## JWT & Token Security\n33\t\n34\t- Algorithm confusion attacks (accepting `none` or `HS256` when expecting `RS256`)\n35\t- Weak or hardcoded secrets\n36\t- Missing expiration (`exp`) or not validating it\n37\t- Sensitive data in JWT payload (tokens are base64, not encrypted)\n38\t- Not validating `iss` (issuer) or `aud` (audience)\n39\t\n40\t## Secrets and PII\n41\t\n42\t- API keys, tokens, or credentials in code/config/logs\n43\t- Secrets in git history or environment variables exposed to client\n44\t- Excessive logging of PII or sensitive payloads\n45\t- Missing data masking in error messages\n46\t\n47\t## Supply Chain & Dependencies\n48\t\n49\t- Unpinned dependencies allowing malicious updates (missing version pins in `requirements.txt`/`pyproject.toml`)\n50\t- Dependency confusion (private package name collision on PyPI)\n51\t- Importing from untrusted sources without integrity checks\n52\t- Outdated dependencies with known CVEs\n53\t\n54\t## CORS & Headers\n55\t\n56\t- Overly permissive CORS (`Access-Control-Allow-Origin: *` with credentials)\n57\t- Missing security headers (CSP, X-Frame-Options, X-Content-Type-Options)\n58\t- Exposed internal headers or stack traces\n59\t\n60\t## Runtime Risks\n61\t\n62\t- Unbounded loops, recursive calls, or large in-memory buffers\n63\t- Missing timeouts, retries, or rate limiting on external calls\n64\t- Blocking operations on the event loop (sync I/O in `async` context)\n65\t- Resource exhaustion (file handles, connections, memory)\n66\t- **`assert` stripped in optimized mode**: `python -O` removes all `assert` statements \u2014 never use `assert` for security/input validation\n67\t- ReDoS (Regular Expression Denial of Service)\n68\t\n69\t## Cryptography\n70\t\n71\t- Weak algorithms (MD5, SHA1 for security purposes)\n72\t- Hardcoded IVs or salts\n73\t- Using encryption without authentication (ECB mode, no HMAC)\n74\t- Insufficient key length\n75\t- Using `==` for secret/token comparison instead of `hmac.compare_digest` (leaks timing information)\n76\t\n77\t## Race Conditions\n78\t\n79\tRace conditions are subtle bugs that cause intermittent failures and security vulnerabilities. Pay special attention to:\n80\t\n81\t### Shared State Access\n82\t- Multiple threads accessing shared variables without locks (GIL does not protect multi-step operations)\n83\t- Global state or module-level mutables modified concurrently\n84\t- Lazy initialization without proper locking\n85\t- Non-thread-safe collections used in concurrent context\n86\t\n87\t### Check-Then-Act (TOCTOU)\n88\t- `if (exists) then use` patterns without atomic operations\n89\t- `if (authorized) then perform` where authorization can change\n90\t- File existence check followed by file operation\n91\t- Balance check followed by deduction (financial operations)\n92\t- Inventory check followed by order placement\n93\t- `tempfile.mktemp()` is deprecated (TOCTOU race) \u2014 use `tempfile.mkstemp()` or `NamedTemporaryFile`\n94\t\n95\t### Database Concurrency\n96\t- Missing optimistic locking (`version` column, `updated_at` checks)\n97\t- Missing pessimistic locking (`SELECT FOR UPDATE`)\n98\t- Read-modify-write without transaction isolation\n99\t- Counter increments without atomic operations (`UPDATE SET count = count + 1`)\n100\t- Unique constraint violations in concurrent inserts\n101\t\n102\t### Distributed Systems\n103\t- Missing distributed locks for shared resources\n104\t- Leader election race conditions\n105\t- Cache invalidation races (stale reads after writes)\n106\t- Event ordering dependencies without proper sequencing\n107\t- Split-brain scenarios in cluster operations\n108\t\n109\t### Common Patterns to Flag\n110\t```python\n111\t# Dangerous patterns:\n112\t\n113\t# TOCTOU\n114\tif not os.path.exists(path):\n115\t    open(path, \"w\").write(data)\n116\t\n117\t# Read-modify-write\n118\tvalue = cache.get(key)\n119\tvalue += 1\n120\tcache.set(key, value)\n121\t\n122\t# Check-then-act\n123\tif user.balance >= amount:\n124\t    user.balance -= amount\n125\t    user.save()\n126\t```\n127\t\n128\t### Questions to Ask\n129\t- \"What happens if two requests hit this code simultaneously?\"\n130\t- \"Is this operation atomic or can it be interrupted?\"\n131\t- \"What shared state does this code access?\"\n132\t- \"How does this behave under high concurrency?\"\n133\t\n134\t## Data Integrity\n135\t\n136\t- Missing transactions, partial writes, or inconsistent state updates\n137\t- Weak validation before persistence (implicit type conversion issues)\n138\t- Missing idempotency for retryable operations\n139\t- Lost updates due to concurrent modifications\n140\t"
  }

EVENT e341.0
{
  "id": "e341.0",
  "source_sequence": 341,
  "session_id": "bcb47d65-8609-4412-b2b2-ea3dba438aa6",
  "actor": "child-of:toolu_01VQ8eGuK63Bv4F6DnXBX3NF",

  "block": {
    "tool_use_id": "toolu_01Xpu41ZYRXdnB9osTKuHam5",
    "type": "tool_result",
    "content": "1\t# SOLID Smell Prompts\n2\t\n3\t## SRP (Single Responsibility)\n4\t\n5\t- File owns unrelated concerns (e.g., HTTP + DB + domain rules in one file)\n6\t- Large class/module with low cohesion or multiple reasons to change\n7\t- Functions that orchestrate many unrelated steps\n8\t- God objects that know too much about the system\n9\t- Circular imports between modules (signals tangled responsibilities \u2014 causes `ImportError`/`AttributeError` at runtime)\n10\t- **Ask**: \"What is the single reason this module would change?\"\n11\t\n12\t## OCP (Open/Closed)\n13\t\n14\t- Adding a new behavior requires editing many `if/elif` chains (or `match/case` blocks)\n15\t- Feature growth requires modifying core logic rather than extending\n16\t- No plugin/strategy/hook points for variation\n17\t- **Ask**: \"Can I add a new variant without touching existing code?\"\n18\t\n19\t## LSP (Liskov Substitution)\n20\t\n21\t- Subclass checks for concrete type via `isinstance` or raises for base method\n22\t- Overridden methods weaken preconditions or strengthen postconditions\n23\t- Subclass ignores or no-ops parent behavior\n24\t- **Ask**: \"Can I substitute any subclass without the caller knowing?\"\n25\t\n26\t## ISP (Interface Segregation)\n27\t\n28\t- ABCs/Protocols with many methods, most unused by implementers\n29\t- Callers depend on broad interfaces for narrow needs\n30\t- `NotImplementedError` stubs in subclasses\n31\t- **Ask**: \"Do all implementers use all methods?\"\n32\t\n33\t## DIP (Dependency Inversion)\n34\t\n35\t- High-level logic depends on concrete IO, storage, or network types\n36\t- Hard-coded implementations instead of abstractions or injection\n37\t- Import chains that couple business logic to infrastructure\n38\t- **Ask**: \"Can I swap the implementation without changing business logic?\"\n39\t\n40\t---\n41\t\n42\t## Common Code Smells (Beyond SOLID)\n43\t\n44\t| Smell | Signs |\n45\t|-------|-------|\n46\t| **Long method** | Function > 30 lines, multiple levels of nesting |\n47\t| **Feature envy** | Method uses more data from another class than its own |\n48\t| **Data clumps** | Same group of parameters passed together repeatedly |\n49\t| **Primitive obsession** | Using strings/numbers instead of domain types |\n50\t| **Shotgun surgery** | One change requires edits across many files |\n51\t| **Divergent change** | One file changes for many unrelated reasons |\n52\t| **Dead code** | Unreachable or never-called code |\n53\t| **Speculative generality** | Abstractions for hypothetical future needs |\n54\t| **Magic numbers/strings** | Hardcoded values without named constants |\n55\t| **Expensive `@property`** | Property getter performs I/O, DB queries, or heavy computation \u2014 callers expect attribute access to be cheap |\n56\t\n57\t---\n58\t\n59\t## Refactor Heuristics\n60\t\n61\t1. **Split by responsibility, not by size** - A small file can still violate SRP\n62\t2. **Introduce abstraction only when needed** - Wait for the second use case\n63\t3. **Keep refactors incremental** - Isolate behavior before moving\n64\t4. **Preserve behavior first** - Add tests before restructuring\n65\t5. **Name things by intent** - If naming is hard, the abstraction might be wrong\n66\t6. **Prefer composition over inheritance** - Multiple inheritance creates MRO complexity and tight coupling\n67\t7. **Make illegal states unrepresentable** - Use types to enforce invariants\n68\t"
  }
}

  "block": {
    "tool_use_id": "toolu_017mKdv1Py89dt318hiT9ACo",
    "type": "tool_result",
    "content": "1\t# Code Quality Checklist\n2\t\n3\t## Contents\n4\t- Error Handling (anti-patterns, best practices, questions)\n5\t- Performance & Caching (CPU, database/IO, caching, memory)\n6\t- Boundary Conditions (None handling, empty collections, numeric, string)\n7\t\n8\t## Error Handling\n9\t\n10\t### Anti-patterns to Flag\n11\t\n12\t- **Swallowed exceptions**: Empty `except` blocks or catch with only logging. Example:\n13\t  ```python\n14\t  try: ...\n15\t  except Exception: pass              # Silent failure\n16\t  try: ...\n17\t  except Exception as e: print(e)     # Log and forget, no re-raise\n18\t  ```\n19\t- **Overly broad except**: Catching bare `Exception` or `BaseException` instead of specific types\n20\t- **Error information leakage**: Stack traces or internal details exposed to users\n21\t- **Missing error handling**: No try-except around fallible operations (I/O, network, parsing)\n22\t- **Async error handling**: Unhandled exceptions in `asyncio` tasks, missing `try/except` in coroutines, fire-and-forget tasks without error callbacks\n23\t\n24\t### Best Practices to Check\n25\t\n26\t- [ ] Errors are caught at appropriate boundaries\n27\t- [ ] Error messages are user-friendly (no internal details exposed)\n28\t- [ ] Errors are logged with sufficient context for debugging\n29\t- [ ] Async task exceptions are retrieved or handled (not silently dropped)\n30\t- [ ] Fallback behavior is defined for recoverable errors\n31\t- [ ] Critical errors trigger alerts/monitoring\n32\t\n33\t### Questions to Ask\n34\t- \"What happens when this operation fails?\"\n35\t- \"Will the caller know something went wrong?\"\n36\t- \"Is there enough context to debug this error?\"\n37\t\n38\t---\n39\t\n40\t## Performance & Caching\n41\t\n42\t### CPU-Intensive Operations\n43\t\n44\t- **Expensive operations in hot paths**: `re.compile` in loops, JSON parsing in hot paths, crypto in loops\n45\t- **Blocking the event loop**: Sync I/O or heavy computation in `async` functions without `run_in_executor`\n46\t- **Unnecessary recomputation**: Same calculation done multiple times\n47\t- **Missing caching**: Pure functions called repeatedly with same inputs, no `@functools.lru_cache` or `@functools.cache`\n48\t- **GIL limitation for CPU-bound work**: `threading` does not parallelize CPU-bound tasks due to the GIL \u2014 use `multiprocessing` or `concurrent.futures.ProcessPoolExecutor`\n49\t\n50\t### Database & I/O\n51\t\n52\t- **N+1 queries**: Loop that makes a query per item instead of batch\n53\t  ```python\n54\t  # Bad: N+1\n55\t  for id in ids:\n56\t      user = db.execute(\"SELECT * FROM users WHERE id = %s\", (id,)).fetchone()\n57\t\n58\t  # Good: Batch\n59\t  users = db.execute(\"SELECT * FROM users WHERE id IN %s\", (tuple(ids),)).fetchall()\n60\t  ```\n61\t- **Missing indexes**: Queries on unindexed columns\n62\t- **Over-fetching**: SELECT * when only few columns needed\n63\t- **No pagination**: Loading entire dataset into memory\n64\t\n65\t### Caching Issues\n66\t\n67\t- **Missing cache for expensive operations**: Repeated API calls, DB queries, computations\n68\t- **Cache without TTL**: Stale data served indefinitely\n69\t- **Cache without invalidation strategy**: Data updated but cache not cleared\n70\t- **Cache key collisions**: Insufficient key uniqueness\n71\t- **Caching user-specific data globally**: Security/privacy issue\n72\t\n73\t### Memory\n74\t\n75\t- **Unbounded collections**: Lists/dicts that grow without limit\n76\t- **Large object retention**: Holding references preventing GC, circular references\n77\t- **String concatenation in loops**: Use `\"\".join(parts)` instead\n78\t- **Loading large files entirely**: Use streaming/iterator patterns instead\n79\t- **Mutable default arguments**: `def foo(items=[])` \u2014 default list is shared across all calls, mutating it causes cross-call contamination\n80\t- **Generator exhaustion**: Iterating a generator/iterator twice silently produces no results on the second pass\n81\t- **Missing context managers**: Not using `with` for files, DB connections, locks \u2014 resources leak on exceptions\n82\t- **Late binding closures**: Lambdas/closures in loops capture the variable by reference, not by value \u2014 all closures see the final loop value\n83\t- **`__del__` unreliability**: `__del__` is not guaranteed to run \u2014 never rely on it for cleanup; use context managers or `atexit`\n84\t\n85\t### Questions to Ask\n86\t- \"What's the time complexity of this operation?\"\n87\t- \"How does this behave with 10x/100x data?\"\n88\t- \"Is this result cacheable? Should it be?\"\n89\t- \"Can this be batched instead of one-by-one?\"\n90\t\n91\t---\n92\t\n93\t## Boundary Conditions\n94\t\n95\t### None Handling\n96\t\n97\t- **Missing None checks**: Accessing attributes on potentially `None` objects (causes `AttributeError`)\n98\t- **Truthiness confusion**: `if value:` when `0`, `\"\"`, `[]`, `{}` are valid values\n99\t- **Excessive None checks**: Deep chains of `if x is not None and x.y is not None` hiding structural issues\n100\t- **`is` vs `==` confusion**: Using `is` to compare values instead of `==` \u2014 works unreliably due to interning (e.g., `x is \"hello\"` may pass for small strings but fail for others)\n101\t- **None vs missing inconsistency**: Mixed usage of `None` and sentinel values without clear convention\n102\t\n103\t### Empty Collections\n104\t\n105\t- **Empty list not handled**: Code assumes list has items\n106\t- **Empty dict edge case**: Iteration or key access on empty dict\n107\t- **First/last element access**: `items[0]` or `items[-1]` without length check\n108\t\n109\t### Numeric Boundaries\n110\t\n111\t- **Division by zero**: Missing check before division (raises `ZeroDivisionError`)\n112\t- **Integer overflow**: Python handles big ints natively, but watch for C-extension or struct boundaries\n113\t- **Floating point comparison**: Using `==` instead of `math.isclose`\n114\t- **Negative values**: Index or count that shouldn't be negative\n115\t- **Off-by-one errors**: Loop bounds, slicing, pagination\n116\t\n117\t### String Boundaries\n118\t\n119\t- **Empty string**: Not handled as edge case\n120\t- **Whitespace-only string**: Passes truthiness check but is effectively empty\n121\t- **Very long strings**: No length limits causing memory/display issues\n122\t- **Unicode edge cases**: Emoji, RTL text, combining characters\n123\t\n124\t### Common Patterns to Flag\n125\t\n126\t```python\n127\t# Dangerous: no None check (AttributeError if user or profile is None)\n128\tname = user.profile.name\n129\t\n130\t# Dangerous: list access without check (IndexError if items is empty)\n131\tfirst = items[0]\n132\t\n133\t# Dangerous: division without check (ZeroDivisionError if count is 0)\n134\tavg = total / count\n135\t\n136\t# Dangerous: truthiness check excludes valid values\n137\tif value:  ...   # fails for 0, \"\", [], {}, False\n138\t```\n139\t\n140\t### Questions to Ask\n141\t- \"What if this is None?\"\n142\t- \"What if this collection is empty?\"\n143\t- \"What's the valid range for this number?\"\n144\t- \"What happens at the boundaries (0, -1, sys.maxsize)?\"\n145\t"
  }
}

Treat newly added tests that assert failure on a previously recovered path as a possible codified regression. The fix is to normalize the input before delegating to the policy owner, which keeps every retry on the normalized key. (Found in preference-reads repo, reader.py, P1, 2026-10-11.)"
